\text{(i) }\tau=\infty,\quad\text{(ii) }s>1/(2\tau),\quad\text{(iii) }\gamma>((2\tau+1)s)/(2\tau(1+s));

Then the excess risk of the spectral algorithm estimator in (13) satisfies

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{2u-\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1}+d^{\ell_{\lambda}-\gamma}+d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{-2\tau u+(2\tau-\tilde{s})\ell_{\lambda}}\right).

On one hand, Proposition
B.1
provides the learning curves for spectral algorithms in the over-regularized regime for certain values of
(\tau,s,\gamma)
. Moreover, since
u^{\prime}<\gamma
(refer to Proposition
F.4
), for any
u\in(0,u^{\prime})
, from Assumption 4 we have
\tilde{\ell}=\ell_{\lambda}
. Therefore, Theorem 3.1 fully recovers the results in Proposition
B.1
.

On the other hand, we note that Proposition
B.1
does not apply to the under-regularized or interpolation regimes, and it does not cover the case where
\tau<\infty
and both
s
and
\gamma
are small. We believe that these excluded cases are of equal importance:

•

As shown in Corollary 3.3 and its subsequent discussion, the benign overfitting phenomenon observed in large-dimensional kernel interpolation and neural networks suggests that the learning curves for small
s
merit further study.

•

The above studies
31
;
25
;
54
;
69
;
58
;
34
that considered the extreme case where
s=0
motivate us to investigate the learning curves for all
s
, to provide a unified explanation of these results.

•

Many large-dimensional datasets in machine learning, especially image datasets, often have dimensions that far exceed the sample size, further encouraging our focus on small
\gamma
.

In summary, it is necessary to refine the conclusions of Proposition
B.1
to derive the learning curves for large-dimensional spectral algorithms.
However, as discussed in Appendix
B.1
, the proof of Proposition
B.1
relies on the validity of approximations such as (
27
) and (
29
), which prevents us from obtaining a complete learning curve.

Finally,
48
determined the exact asymptotic minimax bound for large-dimensional kernel regression problems, known as the Pinsker bound:

Proposition B.2

(Restatement of Theorem 3.1 in
48
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
consist of all the distributions
\rho_{f_{\star}}
on
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
given by (1) such that Assumption
1,2, and 3 hold for some
\gamma,s>0
.
Furthermore, assume that there exists an absolute constant
\alpha>0
, such that we have
n=\alpha d^{\gamma}(1+o_{d}(1))
. Define
p:=\lfloor\gamma/(s+1)\rfloor
.
Then, we have

\inf_{\hat{f}}\sup_{\rho_{f_{\star}}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\sim}\rho_{f_{\star}}^{\otimes n}}\left[\|\hat{f}-f_{\star}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]=\mathcal{C}^{\star}\cdot\left(d^{p-\gamma}+d^{-(p+1)s}\right)(1+o_{d}(1)),

where
\hat{f}
is any estimator of
f_{\star}
, measurable with respect to the observed data set
(X,Y)
, and the definition of the Pinsker constant
\mathcal{C}^{\star}
can be found in Theorem 3.1 in
48
.

Benign overfitting phenomenon of KRR in the interpolation regime

A substantial body of recent work has analyzed large-dimensional KRR in the interpolation regime (i.e., when
\lambda=O_{d}(n^{-1})
), with particular focus on the case
\lambda=0
(i.e., kernel interpolation).
For example,
46
showed the consistency of kernel interpolation with
s=\gamma=1
; subsequent studies
45
;
5
provides upper bounds on the excess risk of kernel interpolation with
\gamma>0
. However, it is usually difficult to derive the matching lower bound, which is not provided in the above studies (also refer to Section 4 in
77
for a detailed discussion).

A tight characterization on the excess risk (with matching upper and lower bounds) of KRR was provided in
1
. They considered a specific type of convolutional kernel on the discrete hypercube and a special form of the true regression function
f_{\star}(x)=x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{L^{*}}
, where
L^{*}
is an absolute constant that formulated in Theorem 1 in
1
.

To our knowledge, the results most similar to Theorem 3.1 are provided by
77
. They determined the exact convergence rates of kernel interpolation stated as follows.

Proposition B.3

(Restatement of Corollary 1 in
77
)
.

Let
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{inter}}(\cdot):=\hat{f}_{0}(\cdot)=\mathscr{K}(\cdot,X)\left(\mathscr{K}(X,X)\right)^{-1}Y
be the kernel interpolation estimator.
Suppose Assumptions 1,2,3, and 6 hold with
s\geq 0
.
Denote
\tilde{s}=\min\{s,2\tau\}
.
Then the excess risk of the kernel interpolation estimator satisfies

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{0}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{\gamma-\ell_{\gamma}-1}+d^{\ell_{\gamma}-\gamma}+d^{-(\ell_{\gamma}+1)s}\right).

It is worth mentioning that the proofs in
77
are difficult to extend to obtain (i) the learning curves for KRR in the over-regularized and under-regularized regimes, or (ii) the learning curves for more general spectral algorithms, even when restricting attention to the interpolation regime.
To illustrate this, let’s restate their proof steps.

They first partition the matrix version of the equality given in (5) into two parts (see Appendix
A
for further details):

K=\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}+\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top},

where
\ell_{\gamma}=\lfloor\gamma\rfloor
, and then showed that

\left\|\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{j}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}-\kappa_{j}\mathrm{I}_{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(\kappa_{j}),\quad\kappa_{j}=\sum_{k=\ell_{\gamma}+1}^{\infty}\mu_{k}^{j}N(d,k),\quad j=1,2;

(31)

and that

\left\|n^{-1}{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}-\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1).

(32)

In the interpolation regime, we have
n\lambda=o_{d}(1)
. Therefore, they established the following approximations for
M:=K+n\lambda\mathrm{I}_{n}
:

\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(M^{-1}\right)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}((n\lambda+1)^{-1})=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{tr}\left(M^{-1}/n\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}((n\lambda+1)^{-1})=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1).

(33)

For the variance term, they decompose it into two parts:

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right)=\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(M^{-1}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M^{-1}\right)~+~\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(M^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M^{-1}\right),

(34)

so that they can use (
31
), (
32
), and (
33
) to obtain the tight bound of the variance.

For the bias term, they decompose it into five parts, separating the contributions from indices
\leq\ell_{\gamma}
and
>\ell_{\gamma}
. By employing the Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury (SMW) formula (see, e.g., Proposition
K.4
), they convert
M^{-1}
into
M_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}:=(\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}+n\lambda\mathrm{I}_{n})^{-1}
, which by (
31
) is approximately the identity matrix and thus easier to handle. For example, for the first term
B_{1,1}
, they obtain

\displaystyle\left\|B_{1,1}\right\|_{2}^{2}:=

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}-\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}M^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\right)\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(35)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ SMW formula }}{=}

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}+\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Similarly, using (
31
), (
32
), and (
33
), they obtained a tight bound on the bias.

However, the following difficulties exist in extending the above results to the two cases we mentioned above:
(i) In the over-regularized and under-regularized regimes, we have
\ell_{\lambda}\leq\ell_{\gamma}
. Consequently, the indices in the decompositions of the variance (
34
) and the bias (
35
) need to be replaced by
\ell_{\lambda}
. Under these circumstances, the preliminary approximations (
31
) and (
33
) no longer hold.
(ii) When KRR is replaced by other spectral algorithms, the matrix
n^{-1}\varphi_{\lambda}^{\mathtt{KRR}}(K/n):=M^{-1}
is replaced by
n^{-1}\varphi_{\lambda}(K/n)
. However, the SMW formula does not necessarily hold for
n^{-1}\varphi_{\lambda}(K/n)
.

B.3
Comparison for the learning curves of kernel regression model and sequence model

Sequence models have been widely considered as simplifications for the analysis of kernel regression models. For example, the celebrated work by
61
and
60
and subsequent ones show that the exact asymptotic of the minimax risks, i.e., the Pinsker bounds, for Gaussian sequence models and fixed-dimensional kernel regression models over
[\mathcal{H}]^{s}
for
s>1
are identical;
14
;
18
;
65
further showed the classical Le Cam equivalence between above kernel regression models and the Gaussian sequence models;

40
;
41
focus on the sequence model and kernel regression model separately, showing that the order of eigen-functions impacts regression outcomes, and that over-parameterization enhances the generalization capability.
Recently,
20
;
57
showed the concentration of the excess risks of KRR in kernel regression models and ridge regression in sequence models.

Proposition 3.4 in Section 3 shows that, under sufficient regularization, the excess risk of large-dimensional kernel regression has the same order as that of the associated sequence model. This complements recent comparison results such as
20
;
57
, which study sharper approximations for KRR under stronger assumptions on the regression function and on the sequence-model noise structure. In contrast, our comparison is derived as a consequence of the full learning-curve theorem and applies uniformly across all
s\geq 0
for the analytic spectral algorithms covered by Definition 2.2.

More specifically, the approximation result of
57
is sharper for KRR when its additional assumptions hold, but those assumptions become restrictive in the large-dimensional regimes covered here. From this perspective, Theorem 3.1 and Proposition 3.4 trade pointwise sharpness for broader regime coverage and a unified treatment of spectral algorithms beyond KRR.

B.3.1
Literature review: Concentration of the excess risks of KRR and ridge regression in sequence models

Recent studies
(
20
;
57
)
have established approximations between
(i) the excess risk of kernel ridge regression (KRR) in the nonparametric regression model (1) and (ii) the excess risk of ridge regression (RR) on the Gaussian sequence model.

Specifically, they first consider the nonparametric regression model (1)
with a regression function
f_{\star}=\sum_{j}f_{j}\phi_{j}\in L^{2}
.
Specifying a kernel
K
with eigenvalues
\lambda_{j}
’s, they then consider the KRR estimator in (11) with regularization parameter
\lambda
.
They also consider the Gaussian sequence model

\tilde{z}_{j}=f_{j}+\tilde{\xi}_{j},j=1,2,\cdots,

where
\tilde{\xi}_{j}\sim_{\text{i.i.d.}}\mathcal{N}(0,[\sigma^{2}+E_{\tilde{\xi}_{j}}[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\tilde{f}_{j}^{\tilde{\lambda}}-f_{j})^{2}]]/n)
.
Here,
\tilde{\lambda}
is the unique non-negative solution of

n\left(1-\frac{\lambda}{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{j}+\tilde{\lambda}},

(36)

and
\tilde{f}_{j}^{\tilde{\lambda}}:=\lambda_{j}\varphi_{\tilde{\lambda}}^{\mathtt{KRR}}(\lambda_{j})\tilde{z}_{j}
is the RR estimator with regularization parameter
\tilde{\lambda}
for
j=1,2,\cdots
.

Remark B.4
.

Notice that the above sequence model is defined implicitly, since the variance of the noise depends on the excess risk, which itself depends on the noise.

57
then built the following approximations on the excess risks of KRR and RR:

Proposition B.5

(Restate Theorem 2 in
57
)
.

Suppose Assumption 1 and 2 hold for some
\gamma>0
.
Further suppose there exists an absolute constant
C
, such that we have
\left\|\mathrm{P}_{>\ell_{\gamma}}f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}\geq\left\|f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}/C
, and for all integers
q\geq 2
, we have
\left\|f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{q}}\leq(Cq)^{(\ell_{\gamma}+1)/2}\left\|f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}
. Then under certain conditions, we have

E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=E_{\tilde{\xi}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\tilde{f}_{j}^{\tilde{\lambda}}-f_{j})^{2}\right]\cdot\left(1+o_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\right),

(37)

where
\mathrm{P}_{>\ell_{\gamma}}
means the projection onto spherical harmonics with degree
>\ell_{\gamma}
, and
E_{\tilde{\xi}}
means taking expectation on the noises
\tilde{\xi}_{j}
,
j=1,2,\cdots
.

With more detailed assumptions on the regression function, Eq. (
37
) provides a sharper approximation on the excess risks than Eq. (21) when the algorithm is specified to KRR. To clarify our novelty, we present a direct comparison:

When
s>0
, Eq. (
37
) relies on assumptions that become increasingly restrictive as
d
grows. Specifically, let
f_{\star}(\cdot)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}f_{j}\phi_{j}(\cdot)
be any regression function satisfying Assumption 3 and with nonzero
L^{2}
norm. Then Proposition 2.1 implies

\left\|\mathrm{P}_{>\ell_{\gamma}}f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{j=N_{\ell_{\gamma}}+1}^{\infty}f_{j}^{2}\leq\mu_{\ell_{\gamma}+1}^{s}\sum_{j=N_{\ell_{\gamma}}+1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}^{-s}f_{j}^{2}\overset{\text{Assumption 3}}{\leq}O_{d}\left(d^{-s(\ell_{\gamma}+1)}\right),

thus requiring that
\left\|f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{q}}^{2}
is of order at most
d^{-s(\ell_{\gamma}+1)}
for any
q=2,3,\cdots
. Therefore, our result applies to a significantly broader class of large-dimensional learning scenarios where the assumptions behind Eq. (
37
) may not hold.

For any regression function in
[\mathcal{H}]^{s}
with
s\geq 0
that satisfies all conditions in Proposition
B.5
, Eq. (
37
) is consistent with Eq. (21). To illustrate this, first notice that we have
\tilde{\lambda}=\Theta_{d}(\lambda)
and
\mathrm{var}(\tilde{\xi}_{j})=\Theta_{d}(\mathrm{var}(\xi_{j}))
: Solving (
36
) we have

\tilde{\lambda}=\left\{\begin{matrix}\left[1+\Theta_{d}(d^{u-\gamma})\right]\lambda=\Theta_{d}(\lambda),&\lambda\gg n^{-1}\\
\Theta_{d}(n^{-1}),&\lambda=O_{d}(n^{-1});\\
\end{matrix}\right.

(38)

and

\sigma^{2}+E_{\tilde{\xi}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\tilde{f}_{j}^{\tilde{\lambda}}-f_{j})^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d}(\sigma^{2});

(39)

Therefore, if (
37
) holds, then it implies (21) since:

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\overset{(\ref{eqn_prop_restate_misia})}{=}\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{\tilde{\xi}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\tilde{f}_{j}^{\tilde{\lambda}}-f_{j})^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(19)\text{ and }(\ref{eqn_mis_implies_ours_2})}{=}

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{\xi}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\hat{f}_{j}^{\tilde{\lambda}}-f_{j})^{2}\right]\right)\overset{(20)\text{ and }(\ref{eqn_mis_implies_ours_1})}{=}\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(E_{\xi}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\hat{f}_{j}^{\max\{\lambda,n^{-1}\}}-f_{j})^{2}\right]\right).

In summary, Theorem 3.1 strictly generalizes Proposition
B.5
,
covering the cases for any
s\geq 0
and for certain analytical spectral algorithms including KRR.

Remark B.6
.

The assumptions in Proposition
B.5
are pivotal to its proof technique and are intrinsically difficult to relax. Notice that the two assumptions,

\left\|\mathrm{P}_{>\ell_{\gamma}}f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}\geq\left\|f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}/C\quad\text{ and }\quad\left\|f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{q}}\leq(Cq)^{(\ell_{\gamma}+1)/2}\left\|f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}\text{ for all integers }q\geq 2,

are sufficient conditions for the convex concentration on eigenfunctions in
20
, which allows them to apply the Hanson-Wright inequality in Lemma 2.1 of
20
.
In contrast, our proof employs a different analytical framework that does not rely on those restrictive premises.

Appendix C
Proof sketch for Theorem 3.1 in a representative regime

To highlight the main ideas without carrying the full case analysis, we focus on the case where

\displaystyle u\in[u^{\prime}(1),\gamma)\text{ (that is, the under-regularized regime), }

(40)

\displaystyle\ell_{\lambda}+1<\ell_{\gamma}<\gamma,\quad s>0,\quad\text{ and }\tau<\infty

This case already contains the main additional difficulty in proving the full learning curve.
Indeed, once
\ell_{\lambda}<\ell_{\gamma}
, the proof must be organized at the cutoff
\ell_{\lambda}
rather than only at
\ell_{\gamma}
.
This creates the intermediate block between degrees
\ell_{\lambda}+1
and
\ell_{\gamma}
, whose contribution must be tracked explicitly, especially in the bias analysis.
This is the key new obstacle that does not appear in the same form in interpolation-level arguments or in proofs restricted to the optimally tuned regime.

The remaining cases are handled by the same general strategy, together with appropriate case-dependent modifications of several known results from the literature.
For example, for the case where
u\in[u^{\prime}(\tau),u^{\prime}(1))
, the bias bound requires a variant of the argument in Appendix D.4.2 of
49
; see Proposition
E.3
.
The complete proof treats all cases systematically.

The proof has three ingredients.
First, we combine kernel approximation results with a decomposition of
K
at the cutoff index
\ell_{\lambda}
.
Second, we use this decomposition to bound the variance and the bias separately.
Third, we compare the resulting exponents to identify the dominant term in each range of
u
.

Key kernel approximation results

We first collect some kernel approximation results, and readers can refer to Appendix
G
for details.

From
31
, we have

\left\|\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{j}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}-\kappa_{j}\mathrm{I}_{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(\kappa_{j}),\quad\kappa_{j}=\sum_{k=\ell_{\gamma}+1}^{\infty}\mu_{k}^{j}N(d,k),\quad j=1,2;

(41)

and that

\left\|n^{-1}{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}-\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1);

(42)

see Lemma
G.1
and Lemma
G.2
.
Recall that we have the decomposition

K=\underbrace{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}}_{K_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}}+\underbrace{\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}}_{K_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}}+\underbrace{\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}}_{K_{>\ell_{\gamma}}}.

Combining the block decomposition of
K
with Weyl’s inequality, we obtain several key properties of the kernel matrices
M:=K+n\lambda\mathrm{I}_{n}
,
K_{>\ell_{\lambda}}
, and
M_{>\ell_{\lambda}}:=K_{>\ell_{\lambda}}+n\lambda\mathrm{I}_{n}
, which will be used throughout the variance and bias analysis.
Specifically, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} &~\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(M\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n\lambda),\quad\mathrm{tr}(M/n)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n\lambda);\\
&~\text{For any }N_{\ell_{\lambda}}+1,\cdots,N_{\ell_{\lambda}+1},\text{ we have }\lambda_{i}(M)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n\lambda);\\
&~\lambda_{\text{max}}(M_{>\ell_{\lambda}})\asymp\lambda_{\text{min}}(M_{>\ell_{\lambda}})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n\lambda),\quad\lambda_{\text{max}}(K_{>\ell_{\lambda}})=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}\right);\\
&~\text{Denote }A=n^{-1}\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{-1}+\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}M_{>\ell_{\lambda}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right),\text{ then }\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(A^{-1}\right)\asymp\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(A^{-1}\right)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n\lambda);\end{aligned}\right.

(43)

see Lemma
G.6
.
For the bias, we also need the following results

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\|\Psi_{>\ell_{\lambda}}\theta_{>\ell_{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(n\|\theta_{>\ell_{\lambda}}\|_{2}^{2}\right);\\
&\|\theta_{>\ell_{\lambda}}\|_{2}^{2}=~\Theta_{d}\left(d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)s}\right);\\
&\left\|\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{-\tau}\theta_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=~\Theta_{d}\left(d^{(2\tau-\min\{s,2\tau\})\ell_{\lambda}}\right);\end{aligned}\right.

(44)

see Lemma
G.7
. Finally, based on the Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury formula, for
k\in\{\ell_{\gamma},\ell_{\lambda}\}
, we have

\displaystyle M^{-1}\Psi_{\leq k}\Sigma_{\leq k}^{1/2}=M_{>k}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq k}\Sigma_{\leq k}^{1/2}\left(\mathrm{I}_{N_{k}}+\Sigma_{\leq k}^{1/2}\Psi_{\leq k}^{\top}M_{>k}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq k}\Sigma_{\leq k}^{1/2}\right)^{-1},

(45)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\leq k}-\Sigma_{\leq k}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq k}M^{-1}\Psi_{\leq k}\Sigma_{\leq k}=\left(\Sigma_{\leq k}^{-1}+\Psi_{\leq k}^{\top}M_{>k}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq k}\right)^{-1};

see Lemma
G.8
.

Bounding the variance

Since
\mathscr{K}(x,X)=\psi(x)^{\top}\Sigma\Psi^{\top}
,
\varphi_{\lambda,K}:=n^{-1}\varphi_{\lambda}(K/n)
, and
\epsilon_{1},\cdots,\epsilon_{n}
are independent with variance
\sigma^{2}
,
the variance term (14) can be decomposed according to the three spectral blocks

>\ell_{\gamma},\qquad\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ldots,\ell_{\gamma},\qquad\leq\ell_{\lambda}.

More precisely,

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle=E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\mathscr{K}(x,X)\varphi_{\lambda,K}\epsilon\right)^{2}\right]=E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\psi(x)^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}\Psi^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\epsilon\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi\Sigma^{2}\Psi^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\underbrace{\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)}_{V_{1}}

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\underbrace{\left(\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\right)}_{V_{2,1}}~+~\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\underbrace{\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right)}_{V_{2,2}}.

We can then bound these three trace terms using (
41
), (
42
), (
43
), and (
45
); see Appendix
D
.

To illustrate the new step, we take
\mathrm{tr}(V_{2,1})
as an example.
This term results from the intermediate block between
\ell_{\lambda}
and
\ell_{\gamma}
, which appears only when
\ell_{\lambda}<\ell_{\gamma}
. Controlling this block is what allows the variance analysis to extend beyond the interpolation-level decomposition.

\mathrm{tr}(V_{2,1})
can be bounded from above as

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(V_{2,1})\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{\leq}C_{2}^{2}n\lambda_{\max}(M^{-2})\cdot\mathrm{tr}\!\left(\frac{\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}}{n}\right)\overset{(*)}{=}O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\!\left(\frac{1}{d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1}n\lambda^{2}}\right),

where the
(*)
step uses the estimates in (
41
)-(
43
), and (
45
).
These same estimates are used repeatedly in the proof of the lower bound as well.
Combining the upper and lower bounds, we conclude that

\mathrm{tr}(V_{2,1})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\!\left(\frac{1}{d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1}n\lambda^{2}}\right)
.

Bounding the bias

To keep the sketch focused, we do not reproduce the full decomposition of
\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})
into the five terms
B_{1},\ldots,B_{5}
; see Appendix
E
.
Most terms can be controlled using the same approximation and estimates in (
41
)–(
45
); see Appendix
E
.
A genuinely new argument is needed for the term

B_{1}:=\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\theta_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}-\theta_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}},

which is the contribution associated with the low-degree block.
Because of the appearance of
\varphi_{\lambda,K}
, this term involves both the intermediate block
K_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}
and the low-degree feature matrix
\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}
.
This interaction is absent in interpolation-only analyses organized at
\ell_{\gamma}
.
Controlling this term is one of the main new steps in the proof.

Using the approximation (
42
) and an algebraic identity in Proposition
J.1
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\underbrace{\left\|\psi_{\lambda}(K/n)Z\theta_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{B_{1,3}}~+\underbrace{\left\|K_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}Z\theta_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{B_{1,4}}~+\underbrace{\left\|K_{>\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}Z\theta_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{B_{1,5}}\right),

where
C=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)
; see (
52
) and (
55
) for detailed calculations.
The term
B_{1,4}
is the most delicate since it involves the interaction matrix
K_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}
.

The key step is to convert
B_{1,4}
into a cross-block Gram term.
Using
K^{-1}K_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}=\mathrm{I}-K^{-1}K_{>\ell_{\lambda}}
, separating the harmless
K_{>\ell_{\gamma}}
contribution, and writing

K_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}=n\tilde{Z}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\tilde{Z}^{\top},\qquad\tilde{Z}:=n^{-1/2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}},

we obtain

B_{1,4}\leq O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\!\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\ell_{\lambda}-2\gamma}\left[n\left\|\underbrace{Z^{\top}\tilde{Z}\,\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\,\tilde{Z}^{\top}Z}_{B_{1,6}}\right\|_{2}+1\right]\right).

This form is useful because it isolates the interaction between the low-degree and intermediate-degree feature matrices in a form that is amenable to concentration.
In particular,
B_{1,6}
is identified as a sum of independent random rank
-
one matrices, and a standard matrix Bernstein inequality shows that
B_{1,6}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
.
This is the crucial estimate behind the control of
B_{1,4}
and the point where the proof goes beyond interpolation-only arguments.

Final comparison of the bias and variance rates

For the regime in (
40
), the previous steps show that the variance contributes two candidate orders, while the bias contributes three candidate orders.
For notational convenience, we denote the corresponding exponents by
v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u)
and
b_{1}(u),b_{2}(u),b_{3}(u)
, respectively; see (
67
).
Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\!\left(d^{v_{1}(u)}+d^{v_{2}(u)}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\leq O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\!\left(d^{b_{1}(u)}+d^{b_{2}(u)}+d^{b_{3}(u)}\right).

Moreover, the lower bound proved in Theorem
E.1
shows that

\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\!\left(d^{b_{1}(u)}\right)\qquad\text{whenever }b_{1}(u)\geq\max\{b_{2}(u),b_{3}(u)\}.

Therefore, the only remaining step is to compare these exponents.
Proposition
F.5
and Proposition
F.6
show that, in the present regime,

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq\max\{b_{2}(u),b_{3}(u)\}.

In other words, the secondary bias exponents
b_{2}(u)
and
b_{3}(u)
never dominate the leading order.
Hence the excess risk is determined by the two variance exponents together with the principal bias exponent
b_{1}(u)
, and we obtain

E_{x,\epsilon}\!\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\!\left(d^{v_{1}(u)}+d^{v_{2}(u)}+d^{b_{1}(u)}\right).

This is the final synthesis step that turns the separate bias and variance bounds into the learning-curve formula in the regime (
40
); see Appendix
F
for the complete case-by-case comparison.

Appendix D
Variance

In this section, our goal is to prove the following theorem, which bounds the variance term in (13).

Theorem D.1
.

Suppose Assumptions 1, 2, and 4 hold.
Then the variance term (14) of the spectral algorithm estimator
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
satisfies

\mathrm{var}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1-2\max\{\gamma-u,0\}}+d^{\tilde{\ell}-\gamma}\right).

Proof.

Because
\mathscr{K}(x,X)=\psi(x)^{\top}\Sigma\Psi^{\top}
and that
\epsilon_{1},\cdots,\epsilon_{n}
are independent with variance
\sigma^{2}
, the variance term (14) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle=E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\mathscr{K}(x,X)\varphi_{\lambda,K}\epsilon\right)^{2}\right]=E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\psi(x)^{\top}\Sigma\Psi^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\epsilon\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi\Sigma^{2}\Psi^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lemma psi psi top}})

\displaystyle=~\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\underbrace{\left(\kappa_{2}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}+\Delta_{2}\right)\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)}_{V_{1}}~+~\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\underbrace{\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)}_{V_{2}},

where
\kappa_{2}=\Theta_{d}\left(d^{-(\ell_{\gamma}+1)}\right)
and
\|\Delta_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)
. We then bound
\mathrm{tr}(V_{1})
and
\mathrm{tr}(V_{2})
separately.

Bounding
\mathrm{tr}(V_{1})
.

On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(V_{1})\leq

\displaystyle n\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(V_{1}\right)=n\kappa_{2}\cdot\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}+\Delta_{2}\right)\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{Prop.~\ref{prop_weyl_ine}})\quad\leq

\displaystyle n\kappa_{2}\cdot\left[\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\right)+\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Delta_{2}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\kappa_{2}\cdot\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\right)\left(1+o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{\leq}

\displaystyle C_{2}^{2}n\kappa_{2}\cdot\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(M\right)^{-2}\left(1+o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lemma lambda max min}})\quad=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\gamma}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}\right);

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(V_{1})\geq

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\right)\left(1-o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{\geq}

\displaystyle C_{1}^{2}\kappa_{2}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(M^{-2}\right)\left(1-o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ast)}{\geq}

\displaystyle C_{1}^{2}\kappa_{2}\cdot n\left[\mathrm{tr}\left(\frac{M}{n}\right)\right]^{-2}\left(1-o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lemma lambda max min}(i)})\quad=

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\gamma}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}\right),

where the third inequality
(\ast)
can be shown using the Jensen’s inequality as in Remark
D.2
.
Therefore,
\mathrm{tr}(V_{1})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\gamma}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}\right)
.

Remark D.2
.

Suppose
A=\text{diag}(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is a positive diagonal matrix.
Then, the Jensen’s inequality
f\left(\frac{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{n}}{n}\right)\leq\frac{f(x_{1})+\cdots+f(x_{n})}{n}
for convex function
f(x)=x^{-2},x>0
implies

\left(\text{tr}\left(\frac{A}{n}\right)\right)^{-2}=\left(\frac{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{n}}{n}\right)^{-2}\leq\frac{a_{1}^{-2}+\cdots+a_{n}^{-2}}{n}=\frac{\text{tr}(A^{-2})}{n}.

Since
M
is positive definite, we can show
(\ast)
with eigendecomposition.

Bounding
\mathrm{tr}(V_{2})
.

We bound
\mathrm{tr}(V_{2})
in two cases: (i)
\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}
, and (ii)
\ell_{\gamma}>\ell_{\lambda}
.

(i) On the one hand, when
\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(V_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\right)\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{=}\Theta_{d}\left(1\right)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M^{-2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}\right)

(46)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma trans in V2}}{=}

\displaystyle\Theta_{d}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\left(\frac{\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}}{n}+\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}\right)^{-1}\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{-2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}\left(\frac{\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}}{n}+\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}\right)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma lambda max min}}{=}

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{(n\lambda+1)^{2}}{n}\right)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}M_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{-2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma lambda max min}}{=}

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{d^{\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}\right),

where the last equality follows from Lemma
G.2
and
G.3
, together with the fact that
N_{\ell_{\gamma}}:=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell_{\gamma}}N(d,k)=\Theta(d^{\ell_{\gamma}})
(see Proposition 2.1).

(ii) On the other hand, when
{\ell_{\gamma}}>\ell_{\lambda}
, similar to (
46
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{d^{\ell_{\lambda}}}{n}\right),

hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(V_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)+\mathrm{tr}\left(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\underbrace{\left(\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\right)}_{V_{2,1}}+\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{d^{\ell_{\lambda}}}{n}\right).

For the upper bound of
\mathrm{tr}(V_{2,1})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(V_{2,1}\right)\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}^{2}n\lambda_{\text{max}}(M^{-2})\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(\frac{\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ Lemma }\ref{lemma lambda max min}}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{(n\lambda+1)^{2}}\right)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(\frac{\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma psi top psi}\text{ and }\ref{lemma psi top psi_int}}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{(n\lambda+1)^{2}}\right)\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left(\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}\right).

Next, we give the lower bound of
\mathrm{tr}(V_{2,1})
.
From Lemma
G.2
and
G.3
, we can denote the SVD decomposition of
\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}
as
\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}=n^{1/2}~OHV^{\top}
, where
O\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
and
V\in\mathbb{R}^{N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)\times N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)}
are orthogonal matrices; and
HH^{\top}=\text{Diag}\{h_{1}^{2}\geq\cdots\geq h_{n}^{2}\geq 0\}
is a diagonal matrix with
h_{1}^{2}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)
and

\displaystyle h_{N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)}^{2}=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1),\quad\text{ if }n>N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1);

(47)

\displaystyle h_{n}^{2}=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1),\quad\text{ if }n\leq N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1).

Denote the SVD decomposition of
\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}
as
\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}=PDP^{\top}
, where
D=\text{Diag}\{d_{1}\geq\cdots\geq d_{n}>0\}
is a diagonal matrix.

Since
\ell_{\gamma}=\lfloor\gamma\rfloor>\ell_{\lambda}
,
N_{\ell_{\lambda}}=\Theta(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})
, and
N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)=\Theta(d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1})
, without loss of generality we assume that

N_{\ell_{\lambda}}+1<\min\{n,N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)\}\leq N_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}.

Recall that we denote
HH^{\top}=\text{Diag}\{h_{1}^{2}\geq\cdots\geq h_{n}^{2}\geq 0\}
and
D=\text{Diag}\{d_{1}\geq\cdots\geq d_{n}>0\}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(V_{2,1}\right)\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left(\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\right)=\mu_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mu_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(OHH^{\top}O^{\top}PDP^{\top}\right)=n\mu_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\left(HH^{\top}O^{\top}PDP^{\top}O\right)

\displaystyle{\geq}

\displaystyle n\mu_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{2}d_{n-i+1}\overset{(\ref{eqn_lower_bound_h_in_variance})}{=}\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{2\ell_{\lambda}+2}}\right){\sum_{i=N_{\ell_{\lambda}}+1}^{\min\{n,N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)\}}}d_{n-i+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{2\ell_{\lambda}+2}}\right){\sum_{i=N_{\ell_{\lambda}}+1}^{\min\{n,N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)\}}}\lambda_{n-i+1}(\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{2})

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{\geq}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{2\ell_{\lambda}+2}}\right){\sum_{i=N_{\ell_{\lambda}}+1}^{\min\{n,N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)\}}}\left[\lambda_{i}(M)\right]^{-2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma lambda max min}}{=}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{2\ell_{\lambda}+2}}\right){\sum_{i=N_{\ell_{\lambda}}+1}^{\min\{n,N(d,\ell_{\lambda}+1)\}}}\left[n\lambda+1\right]^{-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}\right).

where the third line comes from von Neumann’s trace inequality (see also Proposition
K.2
).

Combining all the above, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right)=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\left(\mathrm{tr}(V_{1})+\mathrm{tr}\left(V_{2}\right)\right)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\tilde{\ell}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}+\frac{d^{\tilde{\ell}}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1-2\max\{\gamma-u,0\}}+d^{\tilde{\ell}-\gamma}\right),

and we get the desired results.

\blacksquare

Appendix E
Bias

We first define the following notation:

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime}:=\begin{cases}\tau,&\tau<\infty;\\
s,&\tau=\infty\text{ and }s\geq 1;\\
1,&\tau=\infty\text{ and }s\in\{0\}\cup[\frac{1}{2},1);\\
(2s)^{-1},&\tau=\infty\text{ and }0<s<\frac{1}{2}.\end{cases}

(48)

This notation will be used in the proof of the bias term in the following way: we will apply (9) in the proof for
t=\tau^{\prime}
.
We introduce the finite constant
\tau^{\prime}
to ensure that
the constants implicit in the asymptotic notations
O_{d},o_{d},\Theta_{d},\Omega_{d}
will remain bounded uniformly in
\tau
(even when
\tau=\infty
).

In this section, our goal is to prove the following theorem, which bounds the bias term in (13).

Theorem E.1
.

Suppose Assumptions 1–6 hold.
Then, the bias term (15) of the spectral algorithm estimator
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
satisfies:

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})=

\displaystyle\|B_{1}+B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2},

where

(i)

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2u+(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}}+d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma}\right);

(ii)

If
0<u<\gamma<1
, then for any
\tau\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2\tau^{\prime}u}\right)+\Delta_{1},

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2\tau u}\right)-\Delta_{1},\quad\text{ if }\tau<\infty,

where
0\leq\Delta_{1}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2\gamma}\right)
;

(iii)

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}\right);

(iv)

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{3}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}\right);

(v)

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-(\gamma+\ell_{\gamma}+1)\mathbf{1}\{\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}\}-2(\ell_{\lambda}+1)\mathbf{1}\{\ell_{\gamma}>\ell_{\lambda}\}}\right).

Remark E.2
.

We remark that the upper bound in Theorem
E.1
is not tight in certain cases, for which we will provide a tight bound in Proposition
E.3
.

Proof.

We consider the following two cases: (I) we have
\gamma>\ell_{\gamma}
, or we have
\ell_{\lambda}<\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
, or we have
\ell_{\lambda}\geq\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
and
n\geq 2N(d,\ell_{\gamma})
;
(II) we have
\ell_{\lambda}\geq\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
and
n<2N(d,\ell_{\gamma})
.

Case (I).

Suppose that we have
\gamma>\ell_{\gamma}
, or we have
\ell_{\lambda}<\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
, or we have
\ell_{\lambda}\geq\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
and
n\geq 2N(d,\ell_{\gamma})
.
We first prove that

\lambda_{\text{max}}(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n)\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n).

(49)

Eq. (
49
) can be seen by considering the following:

•

Suppose
\ell_{\lambda}\geq\ell_{\gamma}
. Since
\tilde{\ell}=\ell_{\gamma}
, from Lemma
G.2
and Lemma
G.3
, we have Eq. (
49
) holds.

•

Suppose
\ell_{\lambda}<\ell_{\gamma}<\gamma
. Recall that
\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}=\left[\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}},\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\right]
.
Since
\tilde{\ell}=\ell_{\lambda}
, from Lemma
G.2
, we have

\displaystyle~\left\|\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}\,\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}-\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\lambda}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|\frac{1}{n}\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}\,\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}&\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}^{\top}\,\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\\[10.00002pt]
\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\,\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\lambda}}&\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\,\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\lambda}}}&0\\[5.0pt]
0&\mathrm{I}_{\,N_{\ell_{\gamma}}-N_{\ell_{\lambda}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1),

which implies Eq. (
49
).

•

Suppose
\ell_{\lambda}<\ell_{\gamma}=\gamma
. Similar to the last case, we can use Lemma
G.3
to show Eq. (
49
).

The bias term Eq. (15) can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=E_{x}\left[\left(\mathscr{K}(x,X)\varphi_{\lambda,K}f_{\star}(X)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]

(50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{x}\left[\left(\psi(x)^{\top}\Sigma\Psi^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi\theta-\psi(x)^{\top}\theta\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\Psi^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi\theta-\theta\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi\theta-\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi\theta-\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\underbrace{\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}-\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}}_{B_{1}}+\underbrace{\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}}_{B_{2}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\underbrace{\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}}_{B_{3}}-\underbrace{\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}}_{B_{4}}-\underbrace{\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}}_{B_{5}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Before we bound these terms separately, we need the following calculations.

Denote
Z=n^{-1/2}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}
.
Suppose the SVD decomposition of
Z
is
Z=U(H,0)^{\top}V^{\top}
, where
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
,
V\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{\tilde{\ell}}\times N_{\tilde{\ell}}}
are orthogonal matrices, and
H=\text{Diag}\{h_{1},\cdots,h_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}\}

is a diagonal matrix.
Since Eq. (
49
) holds, we have
h_{1},\cdots,h_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Hence,

\displaystyle Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}=

\displaystyle U\text{Diag}\{HV^{\top}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}VH,0_{n-N_{\tilde{\ell}}}\}U^{\top}

(51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}S_{0}U_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}^{\top}

where we have defined the following

S_{0}=~HV^{\top}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}VH.

Bounding
\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}
.

We consider the following two cases: (B1.i)
\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}
;
(B1.ii)
\ell_{\gamma}>\ell_{\lambda}
.

Proof of case (B1.i).

When
\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}
,
from (
49
) we have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=\left\|\left(\mathrm{I}_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}-\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right)\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(52)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\left\|\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\left(\mathrm{I}_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}-\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right)\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\left\|\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle{=}

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda}(Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}+K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)\right)Z\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle{\leq}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\underbrace{\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-(Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}+K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)\varphi_{\lambda}(Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}+K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)\right)Z\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}}_{B_{1,1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\underbrace{K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}Z\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}}_{B_{1,2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

For
B_{1,1}
:

From Lemma
G.1
, there exist constants
\mathfrak{C}_{5},\mathfrak{C}_{6}>0
such that we have
\mathfrak{C}_{5}\mathrm{I}_{n}\leq K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\leq\mathfrak{C}_{6}\mathrm{I}_{n}
.
Furthermore,
\tilde{\ell}=\ell_{\gamma}
since
\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}
, and
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}})=\mu_{\ell_{\gamma}}=\Omega(d^{-\ell_{\gamma}})
by Proposition 2.1.
Hence, we have
\lambda_{\max}(K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}})\right)
.

Therefore, from (
49
) we have

\displaystyle\|B_{1,1}\|_{2}^{2}

(53)

\displaystyle(\because\psi_{\lambda}(z)\text{ is non-increasing w.r.t }z)\quad\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-(Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top})\varphi_{\lambda}(Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top})\right)Z\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\text{ Eqns. (\ref{eq:Filter_Rem_finite_case3}) and (\ref{eqn_finite_tau_bound})})\qquad=

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\mathrm{I}_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}-S_{0}\varphi_{\lambda}\left(S_{0}\right)\right]HV^{\top}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}VHH^{-1}V^{\top}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}=\mathrm{I}_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}){=}

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{\lambda}\left(S_{0}\right)HV^{\top}\left(\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}VHH^{-1}V^{\top}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\right)\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{\lambda}\left(S_{0}\right)S_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\left\|H^{-1}V^{\top}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because h_{1},\cdots,h_{B_{\tilde{\ell}}}=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1))\qquad\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\psi_{\lambda}\left(S_{0}\right)S_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\left\|\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\text{Eq. (8)})\qquad=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\left\|\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemma~\ref{lemma ge l l2} (iii)})\qquad=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda^{2}d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}}\right);

For
B_{1,2}
:

Eq. (
49
) implies that
\lambda_{\min}(Z^{\top}Z)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1))
and
\lambda_{\max}(Z)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Therefore,

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|B_{1,2}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\right)Z\left(Z^{\top}Z\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(54)

\displaystyle=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\left\|Z\left(Z^{\top}Z\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\left\|\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma}\right),

where

•

the second equality follows from Eq. (
26
);

•

the third equality is because

\lambda_{\max}(Z)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1),~\lambda_{\min}(Z^{\top}Z)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)
;

•

the last equality follows from Lemma
G.7
(iii).

Proof of case (B1.ii).

Suppose
\ell_{\lambda}<\ell_{\gamma}
.

Let
A=Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}
and
B=K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n
. Then
A+B=Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}+K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n=K/n
.
By
Proposition
J.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{n}-Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda}(Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}+K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)

(55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}(K/n)^{-1}\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-(K/n)\varphi_{\lambda}(K/n)\right)+(K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)(K/n)^{-1}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda}(Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}+K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)\right)Z\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}(K/n)^{-1}\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-(K/n)\varphi_{\lambda}(K/n)\right)Z\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|(K_{>\tilde{\ell}}/n)(K/n)^{-1}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\underbrace{\left\|\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}-(K/n)\varphi_{\lambda}(K/n)\right)Z\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{B_{1,3}}

\displaystyle+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\underbrace{\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{B_{1,4}}

\displaystyle+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\underbrace{\left\|K_{>\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{B_{1,5}},

where the first equation follows from the 4th line in Equation (
52
), and the first inequality is because of Equation (
55
) and
\lambda_{\min}(Z^{\top}Z)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1))
.
By the same reasoning as in Eq. (
53
), we can show that
B_{1,3}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda^{2}d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}}\right)
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle B_{1,5}=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|K^{-1}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(56)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\left\|Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\qquad(\because\text{Eq. (\ref{eqn:spe_properties})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\left\|\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\qquad(\because\lambda_{\max}(Z)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1),~~\lambda_{\min}(Z^{\top}Z)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)))

\displaystyle{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma}\right),

where the last equation follows from Lemma
G.7
(iii).

Finally, denote
\tilde{Z}=n^{-1/2}\Psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}
.
Recall that
K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}=Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}
.
Using
K^{-1}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}=\mathrm{I}_{n}-K^{-1}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}
, we have

\displaystyle B_{1,4}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}\|_{2}\leq 1)\quad{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{>\ell_{\gamma}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}K^{-1}\|_{2}\leq 1)\quad{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{>\ell_{\gamma}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemmas }\ref{lemma psi psi top}\text{ and }\ref{lemma ge l l2})\quad{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n^{2}}\left\|K_{\tilde{\ell}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{ Lemmas }\ref{lemma psi top psi}\text{ and }\ref{lemma psi top psi_int})\quad{\leq}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\left\|Z^{\top}\tilde{Z}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\tilde{Z}^{\top}Z\right\|_{2}\left\|(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma ge l l2})\quad{\leq}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma}\left[n\underbrace{\left\|Z^{\top}\tilde{Z}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\tilde{Z}^{\top}Z\right\|_{2}}_{B_{1,6}}+1\right]\right).

It suffices to show that
B_{1,6}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
.

We will use the matrix Bernstein inequality to bound
B_{1,6}
as follows.

For
i=1,\cdots,n
, denote

\displaystyle h_{i}=

\displaystyle~\left[1,\psi_{1,1}(x_{i}),\cdots,\psi_{\ell_{\lambda},N(d,\ell_{\lambda})}(x_{i})\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{\ell_{\lambda}}\times 1}

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{i}=

\displaystyle~\left[\mu_{\ell_{\lambda}+1}\psi_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,1}(x_{i}),\cdots,\mu_{\ell_{\gamma}}\psi_{\ell_{\gamma},N(d,\ell_{\gamma})}(x_{i})\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{(N_{\ell_{\gamma}}-N_{\ell_{\lambda}})\times 1}

\displaystyle A_{i}=

\displaystyle~n^{-1}h_{i}\tilde{h}_{i}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{\ell_{\lambda}}\times(N_{\ell_{\gamma}}-N_{\ell_{\lambda}})}.

By the addition formula (see, e.g., Proposition 1.18 in
28
, page 56 in
31
, or (39) in
48
), we have

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{h}_{i}=\sum_{k=\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{\ell_{\gamma}}\mu_{k}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{N(d,k)}\psi_{k,j}^{2}(x_{i})=\sum_{k=\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{\ell_{\gamma}}\mu_{k}^{2}N(d,k),

(57)

and

\displaystyle\|h_{i}h_{i}^{\top}\|_{2}=h_{i}^{\top}h_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\lambda}}\sum_{j=1}^{N(d,k)}\psi_{k,j}^{2}(x_{i})=\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\lambda}}N(d,k)=N_{\ell_{\lambda}}.

(58)

Therefore, we have
B_{1,6}=\|\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\|_{2}^{2}
, and we can compute
\mathbb{E}A_{i}=~0
,

\displaystyle\|A_{i}\|_{2}=

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\|A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}\|_{2}}=\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\tilde{h}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{h}_{i}\|h_{i}h_{i}^{\top}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle(\because\eqref{eqn_add_on_tilde_h}\text{ and }\eqref{eqn_add_on_h})\quad=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\sum_{k=\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{\ell_{\gamma}}\mu_{k}^{2}N(d,k)N_{\ell_{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle(\because\text{Prop. 2.1})=

\displaystyle~O_{d}(d^{-1/2-\gamma}),

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}]\overset{(\ref{eqn_add_on_tilde_h})}{=}

\displaystyle~n^{-2}\sum_{k=\ell_{\lambda}+1}^{\ell_{\gamma}}\mu_{k}^{2}N(d,k)\mathbb{E}[h_{i}h_{i}^{\top}]=O_{d}(d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)-2\gamma})\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\lambda}}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[A_{i}^{\top}A_{i}]\overset{(\ref{eqn_add_on_h})}{=}

\displaystyle~n^{-2}N_{\ell_{\lambda}}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{h}_{i}\tilde{h}_{i}^{\top}]=\frac{N_{\ell_{\lambda}}}{n^{2}}\Sigma_{\ell_{\lambda}+1,\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}=O_{d}(d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+2)-2\gamma})\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\gamma}}-N_{\ell_{\lambda}}}.

Therefore, from Proposition
K.3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\right\|_{2}\geq n^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2N_{\ell_{\gamma}}\exp\left(-\frac{n^{-1}}{O_{d}(d^{-\ell_{\lambda}-1-\gamma})+O_{d}(d^{-1/2-3\gamma/2})}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(O_{d}(\ell_{\gamma}\log d)-\Theta_{d}(d^{\min(\ell_{\lambda}+1,1/2+\gamma/2}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{d}(1),

and hence
B_{1,6}=\|\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\|_{2}^{2}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
.

Proof of the case
0\leq u<\gamma<1
.

If
0\leq u<\gamma<1
, we have

\ell_{\gamma}=\ell_{\lambda}=0
, from (
51
), we have

\displaystyle H=

\displaystyle h_{1}=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)

(59)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\leq 0}=\mu_{0}=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle S_{0}=

\displaystyle HV^{\top}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}VH=HV^{\top}\Sigma_{\leq 0}VH=\mu_{0}h_{1}^{2}=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}(\lambda).

Following the same derivation in Eq. (
52
) but with the triangle inequality applied in an opposite direction, when
\tau<\infty
we have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\psi_{\lambda}(K/n)Z\theta_{\leq 0}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|B_{1,2}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\psi_{\lambda}(K/n)Z\theta_{\leq 0}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Assumption }5}{\geq}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\psi_{\lambda}(K_{0}/n)Z\theta_{\leq 0}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle{\overset{(\ref{eqn_finite_tau_bound})}{=}}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\psi_{\lambda}(U\text{Diag}\{S_{0},0_{n-1}\}U^{\top})U(H,0)^{\top}V^{\top}\theta_{\leq 0}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle{\overset{(\ref{eq:Filter_Rem_finite_case3})}{=}}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left[\psi_{\lambda}\left(S_{0}\right)HV^{\top}\theta_{\leq 0}\right]^{2}-\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle{\overset{(\ref{eqn_lower_bound_H_S0})}{=}}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left[\psi_{\lambda}\left(\mu_{0}h_{1}^{2}\right)h_{1}\theta_{\leq 0}\right]^{2}-\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle{\overset{(9)\text{ and }(\ref{eqn_lower_bound_H_S0})}{\geq}}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda^{2\tau}\right)\theta_{\leq 0}^{2}-\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda^{2\tau}\right)\left[\Sigma_{\leq 0}^{-\tau}\theta_{\leq 0}\right]^{2}-\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma ge l l2}}{=}

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda^{2\tau}\right)-\Delta_{1}.

where the third last and the second last lines use the fact that
\Sigma_{\leq 0}=\mu_{0}=\Theta_{d}(1)
obtained by Proposition 2.1, and (
54
) implies
0\leq\Delta_{1}=\left\|B_{1,2}\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2\gamma}\right)
.

Similarly to (
52
) and above, for any
\tau\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\psi_{\lambda}(K/n)Z\theta_{\leq 0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle(\because\psi_{\lambda}(z)\text{ is non-increasing w.r.t }z)\quad\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right)\left\|\psi_{\lambda}(K_{0}/n)Z\theta_{\leq 0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(8)}{\leq}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda^{2\tau^{\prime}}\right)+\Delta_{1}.

In summary, we have completed the proof for the following statements about
B_{1}
:

•

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2u+(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}}+d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma}\right);

(60)

•

If
0<u<\gamma<1
, then for any
\tau\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2\tau^{\prime}u}\right)+\Delta_{1};

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2\tau u}\right)-\Delta_{1},\quad\text{ if }\tau<\infty,

where
0\leq\Delta_{1}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-2\gamma}\right)
.

Bounding
\|B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}
.

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}{\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}}

(61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}})(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}})^{-2}(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}){\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right)^{-2}\right\|_{2}\left\|(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}){\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi^{\top}_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right)^{-2}\right\|_{2}\|\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\|_{2}\left\|K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn_bound_eigen_psi_top_psi})}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\left(\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\right)^{\top}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{2}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)\left(\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\cdot\|\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\|_{2}^{2}\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma ge l l2}}{=}O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}\right).

Bounding
\|B_{3}\|_{2}^{2}
.

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{3}\|_{2}^{2}\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma ge l l2}}{=}

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}\right).

(62)

Bounding
\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}
.

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{\top}\left(\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{2}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right)\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn_bound_eigen_psi_top_psi})}{\leq}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(n\|\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\|\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Psi^{\top}_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}Z\left(\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\right)\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(n\|\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)\left\|Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left\|Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma ge l l2}}{\leq}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1}\right)\left\|Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left\|Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn_bound_eigen_psi_top_psi})}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1}\right)\left\|Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\theta_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma ge l l2}}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}+\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1}\right)\left\|Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}Z\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}Z^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1}\right)\left\|K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}.

We derive the bound on
\left\|K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}
in two cases.

Case 1:
When
\tilde{\ell}=\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}
, we have
u\geq\gamma
and

\displaystyle~\left\|K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma psi psi top})\quad{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\left\|K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\left\|\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\text{By Eq.~\eqref{eqn:spe_properties}})\quad{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\left\|M^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma trans in V2})\quad{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\left\|M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\left(\mathrm{I}_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}+\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-2\gamma})\left\|M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\left(\frac{\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}}{n}+\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}/n\right)^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemma \ref{lemma lambda max min} \& Eq.~\eqref{eqn_bound_eigen_psi_top_psi}})\quad{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\gamma})\left\|\left(\frac{\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}}{n}+\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}/n\right)^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\because\text{Lemma \ref{lemma lambda max min} and }u\geq\gamma)\quad{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\gamma})\left\|\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle(\text{Eq.~\eqref{eqn_bound_eigen_psi_top_psi}})\quad

\displaystyle~=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1).

Case 2:
When
\ell_{\gamma}>\ell_{\lambda}
, we have

\displaystyle~\left\|K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma lambda max min}}{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}\right)\left\|\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}\right)\left\|M^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma trans in V2}}{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}\right)\left\|M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\left(\mathrm{I}_{N_{\tilde{\ell}}}+\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{-\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}\right)\left\|M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}\left(\frac{\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}}{n}+\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}/n\right)^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma lambda max min} and (\ref{eqn_bound_eigen_psi_top_psi})}}{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}(n\lambda)^{-2}\right)\left\|\left(\frac{\Sigma_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}}{n}+\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}M_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}/n\right)^{-1}\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lemma lambda max min}}}{=}

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}\right))\left\|\Psi_{\leq\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}\overset{\text{ (\ref{eqn_bound_eigen_psi_top_psi})}}{=}O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left({d^{\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1}}\right).

Combining both cases, we have

\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-(\gamma+\ell_{\gamma}+1)\mathbf{1}\{\ell_{\gamma}\leq\ell_{\lambda}\}-2(\ell_{\lambda}+1)\mathbf{1}\{\ell_{\gamma}>\ell_{\lambda}\}}\right).

(63)

Bounding
\|B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}
.

We have

\displaystyle\|B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\left(\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\right)^{\top}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{2}\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}^{\top}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\right)\left(\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\right)

(64)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{>\tilde{\ell}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|\varphi_{\lambda,K}K_{>\tilde{\ell}}\varphi_{\lambda,K}\|_{2}\cdot\|\Psi_{>\tilde{\ell}}\theta_{>\tilde{\ell}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma ge l l2}}{\leq}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)(s+1)+\gamma}\right)\cdot\|\varphi_{\lambda,K}K\varphi_{\lambda,K}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)(s+1)+\gamma}\right)\cdot\|\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{1/2}\left(\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{1/2}K\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{1/2}\right)\varphi_{\lambda,K}^{1/2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)(s+1)+\gamma}\right)\cdot\|\varphi_{\lambda,K}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn:spe_properties})}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)(s+1)+\gamma}\right)\cdot\|M^{-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lemma lambda max min}}{=}

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{(\tilde{\ell}+1)(s+1)}(n\lambda+1)}\right)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{d^{\tilde{\ell}+1}(\lambda+n^{-1})}\cdot d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eqn:regular_para_upper})}{=}

\displaystyle o_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}\right).

Case (II).

When
\ell_{\lambda}\geq\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
and
n<2N(d,\ell_{\gamma})
,
denote
{N}_{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell_{\gamma}-1}N(d,k)+n/4<N_{\ell_{\gamma}}
and

\Psi_{\leq{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}=\left(\Psi_{1},\cdots,\Psi_{\ell_{\gamma}-1},\left(\Psi_{k,1},\cdots,\Psi_{k,n/4}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times{N}_{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}};

then, Lemma
G.3
implies that

\lambda_{\text{max}}(\Psi_{\leq\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n)\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Psi_{\leq\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n).

Similar to Appendix
A
, we can denote
\Psi_{>{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}
,
\Sigma_{\leq{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}
,
\Sigma_{>{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}
,
\theta_{\leq{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}
,
\theta_{>{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}
,
K_{\leq{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}
, and
K_{>{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}}
.

Notice that we have
{N}_{\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}}=\Theta_{d}(n)=\Theta_{d}(d^{\ell_{\gamma}})=\Theta_{d}(N(d,\ell_{\gamma}))=\Theta_{d}(N_{\ell_{\gamma}})
, hence from Lemma
G.3
, similar to the proof of (iv) in Lemma
G.6
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\text{max}}(M_{>\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n\lambda)\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda_{\text{min}}(M_{>\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}})=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(n\lambda).

Similarly, we can show that Lemma
G.6
(v), Lemma
G.7
, and Lemma
G.8
hold with
\tilde{\ell}
replaced by
\underline{\ell_{\gamma}}
. Therefore, following the above proof for
Case (I)
, we can obtain the same bounds for
\|B_{i}\|
with
i=1,\cdots,5
.

\blacksquare

E.1
Bounds of Bias term in the under-regularized regimes

The following proposition is a modification of Appendix D.4.2 in
49
. It provides a tight bound when
u^{\prime}(\tau)\leq u<u^{\prime}(1)
and
\gamma\geq 1
, where
u^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(\tau)
is the optimal rate of the regularization parameter
\lambda
with qualification
\tau\leq\infty
and coefficient of source condition
s>0
defined in
49
(ignoring the logarithm term).

Proposition E.3
.

Suppose Assumptions 1–6 hold.
Suppose that
\tau\leq\infty
,
s>0
,

u^{\prime}(\tau)\leq u<u^{\prime}(1)
, and
\gamma\geq 1
.
Then, the bias term (15) of the spectral algorithm estimator
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
satisfies:

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq

\displaystyle\Theta_{d}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{-2\tau u+(2\tau-\tilde{s})\tilde{\ell}}\right)+\Delta_{2},

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\geq

\displaystyle\Theta_{d}\left(d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{-2\tau u+(2\tau-\tilde{s})\tilde{\ell}}\right)-\Delta_{2},

where
0\leq\Delta_{2}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1-2\max\{\gamma-u,0\}}+d^{\tilde{\ell}-\gamma}+d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{-2\tau u+(2\tau-\tilde{s})\tilde{\ell}}\right)
.

Proof.

We adopt notations in
49
, and especially:

\displaystyle~\mathcal{N}_{1,\varphi}(\lambda),\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda),\mathcal{M}_{1,\varphi}(\lambda),\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\text{ in (42) of \cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{lu2024saturation}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}

\displaystyle~\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda):=\mathcal{N}_{1,\varphi^{\mathtt{KRR}}}(\lambda)\text{ below (42) of \cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{lu2024saturation}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}

\displaystyle~\tilde{f}_{\lambda},f_{\lambda}\text{ in (36) and (38) of \cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{lu2024saturation}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}.

We also note that the following notations are different in our paper and in
49
;
76
:

•

In
49
,
\ell:=\lfloor u\rfloor
; while in our paper, we denote
\ell_{\lambda}:=\lfloor u\rfloor
.

•

Consider the case where
0<s<1
.
In this case,
\tilde{s}:=\min(s,2\tau)=s
, so the notation
\tilde{p}=\lfloor\gamma/(\tilde{s}+1)\rfloor
used in
76
and in Proposition
F.4
is the same as the notation
p=\lfloor\gamma/(s+1)\rfloor
in
49
.
Notice that one can show that
p=\tilde{p}<\gamma
(see Proposition
F.4
).

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\lambda=d^{-u}
.
We divide the proof into the following three cases: (i)
1\leq s\leq\tau
, (ii)
\tilde{s}:=\min\{s,2\tau\}>\tau
, and (iii)
0<s<1
.

Case (i):
1\leq s\leq\tau
.

Notice that:

•

From (36) and (41) of
49
, we have that
\mathrm{bias}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})
equals to the bias term defined in (41) of
49
.

•

From (60) of
49
, we have that
\mathrm{bias}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq\left\|f_{\lambda}-f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\tilde{f}_{\lambda}-f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}}
and
\mathrm{bias}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\geq\left\|f_{\lambda}-f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}-\left\|\tilde{f}_{\lambda}-f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}}
. Moreover, the latter two terms can be upper bounded separately:

–

From Lemma D.6 and Lemma D.14 in
49
, we have

\left\|f_{\lambda}-f_{\star}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)=\Theta_{d}\left(d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}\lambda^{2\tau}d^{(2\tau-\tilde{s})\ell_{\lambda}}\right).

–

Suppose the following inequalities hold (which we will prove later)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\mathcal{M}_{1,\varphi}^{2}(\lambda)}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right),

(65)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)}{n}\ln(n)(\ln\lambda^{-1})^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{2}\lambda_{i}\varphi_{\lambda}^{2}(\lambda_{i})}{\lambda+\lambda_{i}}f_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right).

Then, on one hand, since
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
, from Lemma 23 in
76
, there exists
\epsilon>0
, such that

n^{\epsilon-1}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)=\Theta_{d}(n^{\epsilon-1}\lambda^{-1})=o_{d}(1);

On the other hand, following the proof of Lemma D.7 in
49
, with (63) and (64) of
49
being
restated as
(
65
),
we have

\left\|\tilde{f}_{\lambda}-f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right).

•

Proposition
F.4
