implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
, hence
\tilde{\ell}=\ell_{\lambda}
.

Therefore, from the above arguments, we only need to verify that (
65
) hold.

Remark E.4
.

We remark that the above argument is the same as that in
49
, where they verify an analogous version of (
65
)
via their Lemmas D.21-D.23.

Since
\tau\geq s
, we have
(\lambda d^{\ell_{\lambda}})^{\tau}\leq(\lambda d^{\ell_{\lambda}})^{s}
, hence from Lemma D.14 in
49
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\mathcal{M}_{1,\varphi}^{2}(\lambda)}{n}

\displaystyle=O_{d}\left(\lambda^{2(s-1)}d^{-\gamma+\ell_{\lambda}s}\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}+\lambda^{-1}d^{-\gamma-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)(s-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\ln(n)(\ln\lambda^{-1})^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{(\lambda)^{2}\lambda_{i}\varphi_{\lambda}^{2}(\lambda_{i})}{\lambda+\lambda_{i}}f_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{d}\left((\ln(d))^{3}\right)\cdot O_{d}\left(\lambda d^{\max\{\ell_{\lambda}(2-s),0\}}+\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}\right),

Denote
\mathbf{I}=\lambda^{2(s-1)}d^{-\gamma+\ell_{\lambda}s}
,

\mathbf{II}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-\gamma-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)(s-1)}
,

\mathbf{III}=\lambda d^{\max\{\ell_{\lambda}(2-s),0\}}(\ln(d))^{3}
,

and
\mathbf{IV}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}(\ln(d))^{3}
. Denote
A_{1}=d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}
,
A_{2}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}
,
A_{3}=d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}
.
Then, (
65
) is implied by the following relationships:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}\right)=

\displaystyle o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right),

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot\left(\mathbf{III}+\mathbf{IV}\right)=

\displaystyle o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right).

•

Recall that Proposition
F.4
implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
. Hence, from Lemma D.14 in
49
, we have that

\displaystyle~~n\cdot\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\cdot\Theta_{d}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}+\frac{d^{\ell_{\lambda}}}{n}+d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}\lambda^{2\tau}d^{(2\tau-\tilde{s})\ell_{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d}(A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}).

•

We have

\mathbf{I}=d^{-2u(s-1)-\gamma+\ell_{\lambda}s}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}d^{-u(s-1)-\gamma}d^{(\ell_{\lambda}-u)(s-1)}=o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2}).

•

Proposition
F.4
implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
, hence

\mathbf{II}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}d^{u-\gamma}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)+1}\overset{s\geq 1}{=}o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2}).

•

When
s\geq 2
, we have

\mathbf{III}=d^{-u}(\ln(d))^{3}\overset{u>0}{=}o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2});

When
s<2
, we have

\mathbf{III}=d^{-u+\ell_{\lambda}(2-s)}(\ln(d))^{3}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}d^{-u-\ell_{\lambda}(s-1)}(\ln(d))^{3}=o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2}).

•

Proposition
F.4
implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
, hence

\mathbf{IV}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}(\ln(d))^{3}=o_{d}(d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)})=o_{d}(A_{3}).

Combining all these, we obtained (
65
).

Case (ii):
\tilde{s}:=\min\{s,2\tau\}>\tau
.
Similar to
Case (i)
, we only need to show that (
65
) holds.
From Lemma D.14 in
49
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\mathcal{M}_{1,\varphi}^{2}(\lambda)}{n}=O_{d}\left(\lambda^{2(\tau-1)}d^{-\gamma+\ell_{\lambda}(2\tau-\tilde{s})}+\lambda^{-1}d^{-\gamma-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)(s-1)}\mathbf{1}\{s\leq 2\tau\}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\ln(n)(\ln\lambda^{-1})^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{(\lambda)^{2}\lambda_{i}\varphi_{\lambda}^{2}(\lambda_{i})}{\lambda+\lambda_{i}}f_{i}^{2}=O_{d}\left((\ln(d))^{3}\right)\cdot O_{d}\left(\lambda d^{\max\{\ell_{\lambda}(2-s),0\}}+\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}\right).

Denote
\mathbf{I}=\lambda^{2(\tau-1)}d^{-\gamma+\ell_{\lambda}(2\tau-\tilde{s})}
,

\mathbf{II}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-\gamma-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)(s-1)}
,

\mathbf{III}=\lambda d^{\max\{\ell_{\lambda}(2-s),0\}}(\ln(d))^{3}
,

and
\mathbf{IV}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}(\ln(d))^{3}
.
Denote
A_{1}=d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}
,
A_{2}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}
,
A_{3}=d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}
.
Then, (
65
) is implied by the following relationships:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}\right)=

\displaystyle o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right)

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot\left(\mathbf{III}+\mathbf{IV}\right)=

\displaystyle o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right)

•

Recall that Proposition
F.4
implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
. Hence, from Lemma D.14 in
49
, we have that

\displaystyle~n\cdot\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\cdot\Theta_{d}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}+\frac{d^{\ell_{\lambda}}}{n}+d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}\lambda^{2\tau}d^{(2\tau-\tilde{s})\ell_{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d}(A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}).

•

When
s>2\tau
, we have

\mathbf{I}=\lambda^{2(\tau-1)}d^{-\gamma}{=}o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2});

When
\tau<s\leq 2\tau
, we have
(\lambda d^{\ell_{\lambda}})^{2\tau}\leq(\lambda d^{\ell_{\lambda}})^{s}
, hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}=

\displaystyle(\lambda d^{\ell_{\lambda}})^{2\tau}d^{2u-\gamma-s\ell_{\lambda}}=O_{d}(d^{2u-us-\gamma})=O_{d}(d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1})O_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}-us}d^{1-\gamma})

\displaystyle(\because\gamma\geq 1,~s>1)\quad=

\displaystyle O_{d}(d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1})o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}-s})=o_{d}(A_{1}).

•

Same as the proof in
Case (i)
, we have

\mathbf{II},\mathbf{III},\mathbf{IV}=o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}}+d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)})=o_{d}(A_{2}+A_{3}).

Combining all these, we obtained (
65
).

Case (iii):
0<s<1
.

The proof is based on the following argument, which is similar to the one in
Case (i)
:

Suppose there exists a constant
\epsilon>0
only depending on
s
and
\gamma
, such that the following inequalities hold (which we will prove later)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)}{n}\ln(n)(\ln\lambda^{-1})^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{2}\lambda_{i}\varphi_{\lambda}^{2}(\lambda_{i})}{\lambda+\lambda_{i}}f_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right);

(66)

\displaystyle n^{-2}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\left(\left\|f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+n^{1-s+\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right).

Then, following the proof of Lemma D.8 in
49
, where (75) and (76) therein are restated as (
66
), we obtain the desired bounds for the bias term
\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})
.

Therefore, we only need to establish (
66
).

Proposition
F.4
implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
, hence from Lemma D.14 in
49
and Lemma 23 in
76
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\ln(n)(\ln\lambda^{-1})^{2}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{(\lambda)^{2}\lambda_{i}\varphi_{\lambda}^{2}(\lambda_{i})}{\lambda+\lambda_{i}}f_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{d}\left((\ln(d))^{3}\right)\cdot O_{d}\left(\lambda d^{\ell_{\lambda}(2-s)}+\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}\right)

\displaystyle n^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)n^{1-s}

\displaystyle=O_{d}\left(\lambda^{-1}d^{-\gamma s}\right).

Moreover, notice that (8) implies
\varphi_{\lambda}(z)\leq C_{2}(z+\lambda)^{-1}
. Hence, similar to the proof of Lemma 27 in
76
(for kernel ridge regression estimator), we can show that

\displaystyle n^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)\left\|f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}=

\displaystyle~O_{d}\left(d^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1}\left\|f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O_{d}\left(d^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1}\left[d^{\frac{(1-s)\ell_{\lambda}}{2}}+\lambda^{-1}d^{-\frac{(1+s)(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}{2}}\right]\right).

Denote
\mathbf{I}=\lambda d^{\ell_{\lambda}(2-s)}(\ln(d))^{3}
,

\mathbf{II}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}(\ln(d))^{3}
,

\mathbf{III}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-\gamma s}
,

\mathbf{IV}=d^{u-\gamma+\frac{(1-s)\ell_{\lambda}}{2}}
,
and
\mathbf{V}=d^{2u-\gamma-\frac{(1+s)(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}{2}}
.
Denote
A_{1}=d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}
,
A_{2}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}
,
A_{3}=d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}
.

Note that (
66
) is implied by the following inequalities:

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}\right)=

\displaystyle o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right)

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot\left(\mathbf{III}+\mathbf{IV}+\mathbf{V}\right)=

\displaystyle o_{d}\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right)

•

Recall that Proposition
F.4
implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
. Hence, from Lemma D.14 in
49
, we have that

\displaystyle~n\cdot\left(\mathcal{M}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\mathcal{N}_{2,\varphi}(\lambda)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n\cdot\Theta_{d}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\ell_{\lambda}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}+\frac{d^{\ell_{\lambda}}}{n}+d^{-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}\lambda^{2\tau}d^{(2\tau-\tilde{s})\ell_{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d}(A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}).

•

We have

\mathbf{I}=d^{-u+\ell_{\lambda}(2-s)}(\ln(d))^{3}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}d^{-u+\ell_{\lambda}(1-s)}(\ln(d))^{3}\overset{s>0}{=}o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2}).

•

Proposition
F.4
implies that
u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma
, hence

\mathbf{II}=\lambda^{-1}d^{-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}(\ln(d))^{3}=o_{d}(d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)})=o_{d}(A_{3}).

•

For
\mathbf{III}
, we analyze three possible cases:

–

Suppose
u-\ell_{\lambda}<\gamma s
. Then

\mathbf{III}=O_{d}(d^{u-\gamma s})=O_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}}d^{u-\ell_{\lambda}-\gamma s})=o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2}).

–

Suppose
u-\ell_{\lambda}\geq\gamma s
and
s>1/2
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{III}

\displaystyle=O_{d}(d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}d^{u-\gamma s-2u+\ell_{\lambda}+1})

\displaystyle=O_{d}(d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}d^{-2\gamma s+1})

\displaystyle\overset{\gamma\geq 1,s>1/2}{=}o_{d}(d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1})=o_{d}(A_{1}).

–

Suppose
u-\ell_{\lambda}\geq\gamma s
and
s\leq 1/2
. We first prove by contradiction that
\ell_{\lambda}<\ell_{\gamma}
: Suppose that
\ell_{\lambda}=\ell_{\gamma}
, then from
Case III
in the proof of Proposition
F.4
with
\tau=1
and
\tilde{p}=p=\lfloor\gamma/(s+1)\rfloor
, we have

p:=\lfloor\gamma/(s+1)\rfloor\leq\ell_{\gamma}=\ell_{\lambda}\overset{u<u^{\prime}(1)}{\leq}p,

implying that
\ell_{\lambda}=p
. Hence, from
Case III
in the proof of Proposition
F.4
with
\tilde{p}=p
, we further have

\displaystyle~\gamma s\leq u-\ell_{\lambda}=u-p

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\{\begin{matrix}\frac{\gamma-p-sp}{2}\leq\frac{s}{2}<\gamma s,&p\geq 1\text{ and }p(s+1)\leq\gamma<ps+p+s;\\
\frac{s}{2}<\gamma s,&p\geq 0\text{ and }ps+p+s\leq\gamma<ps+p+s+1,\end{matrix}\right.

leading to a contradiction. (Notice that
p(s+1)\leq\gamma<ps+p+s
with
p=0
is not allowed since
\gamma\geq 1>s
). Therefore, we have
\ell_{\lambda}<\ell_{\gamma}
, and hence

\mathbf{III}=O_{d}(d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}d^{u-\gamma s-\gamma+s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)})=O_{d}(d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}d^{u-\gamma})=o_{d}(d^{\gamma-s(\ell_{\lambda}+1)})=o_{d}(A_{3}).

•

We have

\mathbf{IV}=d^{\ell_{\lambda}}d^{u-\gamma+\frac{(1-s)\ell_{\lambda}}{2}-\ell_{\lambda}}\overset{u<\gamma,s<1}{=}o_{d}(d^{\ell_{\lambda}})=o_{d}(A_{2}).

•

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}=

\displaystyle~d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}d^{2u-\gamma-\frac{(1+s)(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}{2}-2u+\ell_{\lambda}+1}=d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}d^{-\gamma+\frac{(1-s)(\ell_{\lambda}+1)}{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{0<s<1}{=}

\displaystyle~o_{d}(d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1}d^{-\frac{2\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1)}{2}})=o_{d}(d^{2u-\ell_{\lambda}-1})=o_{d}(A_{1}).

Combining all the above steps, we obtained (
66
).

\blacksquare

Appendix F
Learning curve

In this section, we will use Theorem
D.1
, Theorem
E.1
, Proposition
B.1
, and Proposition
E.3
to determine the convergence rate of the excess risk in (13).

F.1
Notations and restatement of results

We first introduce some notations.

Recall that
u
is an absolute constant given in Assumption 4, and we have
\lambda=\Theta_{d}\left(d^{-u}\right)
. Denote

\displaystyle v_{1}=v_{1}(u)=\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1-2\max\{\gamma-u,0\},

(67)

\displaystyle v_{2}=v_{2}(u)=\tilde{\ell}-\gamma,

\displaystyle b_{1}=b_{1}(u)=-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s,

\displaystyle b_{2}=b_{2}(u,t)=-2tu+(2t-\min\{s,2t\})\tilde{\ell},\quad 1\leq t<\infty,

\displaystyle v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{1}^{\prime}(u)=\gamma-\ell_{\lambda}-1-2(\gamma-u),

\displaystyle v_{2}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}(u)=\ell_{\lambda}-\gamma,

\displaystyle b_{1}^{\prime}=b_{1}^{\prime}(u)=-(\ell_{\lambda}+1)s,

\displaystyle b_{2}^{\prime}=b_{2}^{\prime}(u,t)=-2tu+(2t-\min\{s,2t\})\ell_{\lambda},\quad 1\leq t<\infty,

\displaystyle b_{3}=b_{3}(u)=(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-2\gamma,

\displaystyle b_{4}=b_{4}(u)=(2-\min\{s,2\})\tilde{\ell}-\gamma-\tilde{\ell}-1.

Furthermore, when
s>0
,
let
u^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(\tau)
be the optimal rate of the regularization parameter
\lambda
with qualification
\tau\leq\infty
defined in
49
(we will also describe
u^{\prime}
in the proof of Proposition
F.4
).

•

Let’s first restate results in Theorem
D.1
and Theorem
E.1
with notations defined in (
67
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)

(68)

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})=

\displaystyle\|B_{1}+B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{2}(u,1)}+d^{b_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\|B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\|B_{3}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\ell_{\gamma}+1>\gamma\text{ and }\ell_{\lambda}+1>u)\quad\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle o_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{2}(u,1)}+d^{b_{3}}\right);

Moreover, if
0<u<\gamma<1
, we have
\tilde{\ell}:=\min\{\ell_{\gamma},\ell_{\lambda}\}=0
.
Therefore, for any
\tau\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{2}(u,\tau^{\prime})}\right)+\Delta_{1}

(69)

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right)-\Delta_{1},\quad\text{ if }\tau<\infty

\displaystyle\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{-\gamma-1}\right)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{4}}\right),

where
\tau^{\prime}
is defined as in (
48
), and
0\leq\Delta_{1}=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{3}}\right)
.

•

We next restate the results in Proposition
B.1
with notations defined in (
67
). Suppose all assumptions and conditions in Proposition
B.1
hold; this implies that
\tau\leq\infty
,
s>0
,
u\in(0,u^{\prime}(\tau))
, and at least one of the following conditions hold:
\text{(i) }\tau=\infty,\quad\text{(ii) }s>1/(2\tau),\quad\text{(iii) }\gamma>((2\tau+1)s)/(2\tau(1+s))
).
Then for any
u<u^{\prime}(\tau)
, we have

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]

(70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}^{\prime}}+d^{v_{2}^{\prime}}+d^{b_{1}^{\prime}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}^{\prime}(u,\tau)}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{ Proposition \ref{prop_e_1}})\quad=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

•

Finally, let’s restate results in Proposition
E.3
with notations defined in (
67
). Suppose all assumptions and conditions in Proposition
E.3
hold; this implies that
\tau\leq\infty
,
s>0
,
u^{\prime}(\tau)\leq u<u^{\prime}(1)
, and
\gamma\geq 1
).
Then for any
u^{\prime}(\tau)\leq u<u^{\prime}(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq

\displaystyle\Theta_{d}\left(d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right)+\Delta_{2}

(71)

\displaystyle\mathrm{bias}^{2}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\geq

\displaystyle\Theta_{d}\left(d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right)-\Delta_{2};

where
0\leq\Delta_{2}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right)

In the next two subsections, we will consider the case with
s=0
in Appendix
F.2
and with
s>0
in Appendix
F.3
.

F.2
s=0

When
s=0
, Theorem
D.1
and Theorem
E.1
together imply the following results:

Corollary F.1
.

Suppose Assumptions 1,2,3,4,5, and 6 hold with
\tau\leq\infty
and
s=0
. Then, the excess risk of the spectral algorithm estimator in (13) satisfies

E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right).

Proof.

Notice that
b_{1}=0
and these four quantities are non-positive:
v_{1},v_{2},b_{2}(u,1),b_{3}
. Hence (
68
) implies that

E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,1)}+d^{b_{3}}\right)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right),

and that

E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\|B_{3}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}-\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(1\right),

and we obtain the desired results.

\blacksquare

F.3
s>0

We will prove the following results.

Corollary F.2
.

Suppose Assumptions 1,2,3,4,5, and 6 hold with
s>0
.
Denote
\tilde{s}=\min\{s,2\tau\}
.
Assume in addition that either
\tau=\infty
, or
s>1/(2\tau)
, or
\gamma>((2\tau+1)s)/(2\tau(1+s))
.
Then the excess risk of the spectral algorithm estimator in (13) satisfies

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\tilde{\ell}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}+\frac{d^{\tilde{\ell}}}{n}+d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}\lambda^{2\tau}d^{(2\tau-\tilde{s})\tilde{\ell}}\right).

Corollary F.3
.

Suppose Assumptions 1,2,3,4,5, and 6 hold with
s>0
.
Denote
\tilde{s}=\min\{s,2\tau\}
.
Assume in addition that both
\tau<\infty
, and
s\leq 1/(2\tau)
, and
\gamma\leq((2\tau+1)s)/(2\tau(1+s))
.
Then, the excess risk of the spectral algorithm estimator in (13) satisfies

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{n}{d^{\tilde{\ell}+1}(n\lambda+1)^{2}}+\frac{d^{\tilde{\ell}}}{n}+d^{-(\tilde{\ell}+1)s}+\lambda^{2\tau}d^{(2\tau-\tilde{s})\tilde{\ell}}\right).

Proof of Corollary
F.2
.

Let’s separate the proof into four parts:

(i)

u<u^{\prime}(\tau)
;

(ii)

u^{\prime}(\tau)\geq u^{\prime}(1)
and
u\geq u^{\prime}(\tau)
;

(iii)

u^{\prime}(\tau)<u^{\prime}(1)
and
u\geq u^{\prime}(1)
;

(iv)

u^{\prime}(\tau)<u^{\prime}(1)
and
u\in[u^{\prime}(\tau),u^{\prime}(1))
.

Case (i).
Suppose
u<u^{\prime}(\tau)
. (
70
) implies

\displaystyle~E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right).

Case (ii).
Suppose
u^{\prime}(\tau)\geq u^{\prime}(1)
. For any
u\geq u^{\prime}(\tau)\geq u^{\prime}(1)
, from Proposition
F.5
and Proposition
F.6
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq\max\{\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}b_{2}(u,\tau),b_{2}(u,1),b_{3}(u)\},

Therefore from (
68
) we have

•

Upper bound:

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

•

Lower bound:

\displaystyle~E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right).

Case (iii).
Suppose
u^{\prime}(\tau)<u^{\prime}(1)
. For any
u\geq u^{\prime}(1)\geq u^{\prime}(\tau)
, from Proposition
F.5
and Proposition
F.6
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq\max\{\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}b_{2}(u,\tau),b_{2}(u,1),b_{3}(u)\},

Therefore, as in
Case (ii)
, we can show that

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right).

Case (iv).
Suppose
u^{\prime}(\tau)<u^{\prime}(1)
and
u\in[u^{\prime}(\tau),u^{\prime}(1))
. From Proposition
F.5
and Proposition
F.6
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq\max\{\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}b_{2}(u,\tau),b_{3}(u),b_{4}(u)\}.

(72)

Therefore,

(a)

When
\gamma\geq 1
, from the variance bound in (
68
) and the bias bound in (
71
), we have

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+\mathbf{1}\{\tau<\infty\}d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

(b)

When
\gamma<1
and
\tau<\infty
, Proposition
F.4
with
t=1
further implies
0<u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma<1
. Hence from (
68
) and (
69
) we have

–

Upper bound:

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\leq

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{3}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}+d^{b_{3}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{ (\ref{eqn_tau_finite_b234_bound})})\quad=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

–

Lower bound:

*

If
b_{1}>b_{4}
, then

\displaystyle~E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

*

If
b_{1}\leq b_{4}
, since Proposition
F.5
implies that
b_{4}<v_{2}
, hence (
72
) implies

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u)\}\geq\max\{b_{1}(u),b_{2}(u,\tau)\}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right),

(c)

Finally, suppose
\gamma<1
and
\tau=\infty
. Recall that from (
48
), we have

\tau^{\prime}:=\left\{\begin{matrix}s,&s\geq 1;\\
1,&s\in[\frac{1}{2},1);\\
(2s)^{-1},&0<s<\frac{1}{2}.\end{matrix}\right.

Therefore, from Proposition
F.4
we have

\displaystyle u^{\prime}(\infty)=\left\{\begin{matrix}u^{\prime}(s),&\quad s\geq 1;\\
\gamma/2=u^{\prime}(1)&\quad s\in[\frac{1}{2},1)\text{ and }\gamma<s;\\
\min\{\gamma(1+s)-s,\gamma/2\}\geq\frac{s}{2}=u^{\prime}(1)&\quad s\in[\frac{1}{2},1)\text{ and }s\leq\gamma<1;\\
\gamma s=\frac{\gamma}{2(2s)^{-1}}=u^{\prime}((2s)^{-1})&\quad 0<s<\frac{1}{2}\text{ and }\gamma<s;\\
\min\{\gamma(1+s)-s,\gamma/2\}\geq s^{2}=\frac{s}{2(2s)^{-1}}=u^{\prime}((2s)^{-1})&\quad 0<s<\frac{1}{2}\text{ and }s\leq\gamma<1;\end{matrix}\right.

implying that
u^{\prime}(\infty)\geq u^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})
.
Furthermore, the assumption of
Case (iv)
implies
u\geq u^{\prime}(\infty)\geq u^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})
.
Therefore, from Proposition
F.5
and Proposition
F.6
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq\max\{b_{2}(u,\tau^{\prime}),b_{3}(u),b_{4}(u)\}.

From the assumption of
Case (iv)
, we have

u<u^{\prime}(1)
. From Proposition
F.4
we have
0<u<u^{\prime}(1)<\gamma<1
.

Hence from (
68
) and (
69
) we have

–

Upper bound:

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\leq

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{3}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{2}(u,\tau^{\prime})}+d^{b_{3}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right);

–

Lower bound:

*

If
b_{1}>b_{4}
, then

\displaystyle~E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right);

*

If
b_{1}\leq b_{4}
, since Proposition
F.5
implies that
b_{1}\leq b_{4}<v_{2}
, hence we have

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right).

Combining all above cases, we obtain the desired results.

\blacksquare

Proof of Corollary
F.3
.

Let’s separate the proof into two parts:

(i)

u<\gamma
;

(ii)

u\geq\gamma
.

Case (i).
Suppose
u<\gamma
. Since
s\leq 1/(2\tau)
and

\gamma\leq\frac{2\tau+1}{2\tau}\frac{s}{s+1}\leq\frac{2\tau+1}{2\tau}\frac{\frac{1}{2\tau}}{\frac{1}{2\tau}+1}<1,

hence we have
0<u<\gamma<1
. Therefore,
from (
68
) and (
69
)
we have

•

Upper bound:

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\leq

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{3}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}+\|B_{4}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}+d^{b_{3}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle(\because\text{ Proposition \ref{prop_e_2}})\quad=

\displaystyle~O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

•

Lower bound:

–

If
b_{1}>b_{4}
and
b_{1}\geq b_{2}(u,\tau)
, then

\displaystyle~E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{b_{1}>b_{4}}{=}

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

–

If
b_{1}\leq b_{4}
and
b_{1}\geq b_{2}(u,\tau)
, since Proposition
F.5
implies that
b_{4}<v_{2}
, we have
\max\{b_{1},b_{2}(u,\tau)\}<v_{2}
, hence

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right),

–

If
b_{2}(u,\tau)>b_{3}
and
b_{1}<b_{2}(u,\tau)
, then

\displaystyle~E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{1}+B_{2}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{b_{2}(u,\tau)>\max\{b_{1},b_{3}\}}{=}

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{2}}(u,\tau)\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right),

–

If
b_{2}(u,\tau)\leq b_{3}
and
b_{1}<b_{2}(u,\tau)
, since Proposition
F.5
implies that
b_{3}\leq v_{2}
, we have
\max\{b_{1},b_{2}(u,\tau)\}\leq v_{2}
, hence

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right).

Case (ii).
Suppose
u\geq\gamma
. From Proposition
F.4
with
t=1
and
t=\tau
, we have
\gamma>u^{\prime}(1)
and
\gamma>u^{\prime}(\tau)
, hence from Proposition
F.5
and Proposition
F.6
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq\max\{b_{2}(u,1),b_{2}(u,\tau),b_{3}(u)\},

Therefore, from (
68
) we have

•

Upper bound:

\displaystyle E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right)=O_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right);

•

Lower bound:

\displaystyle~E_{x,\epsilon}\left[\left(\hat{f}_{\lambda}(x)-f_{\star}(x)\right)^{2}\right]\geq\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}\right)+\|B_{3}-B_{4}-B_{5}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}\right)=\Omega_{d,\mathbb{P}}\left(d^{v_{1}}+d^{v_{2}}+d^{b_{1}}+d^{b_{2}(u,\tau)}\right).

Putting the two bounds together yields the desired result.

\blacksquare

F.4
Quantities calculation of learning curve results

Proposition F.4
.

Let
u^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(t)
be the optimal rate of the regularization parameter
\lambda
with qualification
t\in[1,\infty]
and coefficient of source condition
s>0
defined in
49
(ignoring the logarithm term). Then, we have
u^{\prime}<\gamma
and
\tilde{p}\leq u^{\prime}<\tilde{p}+1
, where
\tilde{s}:=\min\{s,2t\}
and
\tilde{p}:=\lfloor\gamma/(\tilde{s}+1)\rfloor
.

As a direct corollary, for any
u\leq u^{\prime}
, we have
\ell_{\lambda}=\lfloor u\rfloor\leq\ell_{\gamma}
, hence

v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{1},~v_{2}^{\prime}=v_{2},~b_{1}^{\prime}=b_{1},~b_{2}^{\prime}(u,t)=b_{2}(u,t).

Proof.

According to Corollaries D.15–17 in
49
, we divide the proof into four cases.

Case I:
1\leq s\leq t

Denote
p:=\lfloor\gamma/(s+1)\rfloor
.

(1.1) Suppose
p\geq 1
and
p(s+1)\leq\gamma<ps+p+s
. We have

u^{\prime}=p+\frac{1}{2}<p+ps\leq\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad p\leq u^{\prime}<p+1.

(1.2) Suppose
p\geq 1
and
ps+p+s\leq\gamma<ps+p+s+1
. We have

u^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma-(p+1)(s-1)}{2}<p+1<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad p\leq u^{\prime}<p+1.

(1.3) Suppose
\gamma<s
. We have

u^{\prime}=\min\{\gamma,1\}/2<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad 0\leq u^{\prime}<1.

(1.4) Suppose
s\leq\gamma<s+1
. We have

u^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma-(s-1)}{2}<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad 0\leq u^{\prime}<1.

Case II:
\tilde{s}>t

Denote
\tilde{p}:=\lfloor\gamma/(\tilde{s}+1)\rfloor
.

(2.1) Suppose
\gamma\geq 1
and
0\leq\gamma-\tilde{p}(\tilde{s}+1)<t
. We have

u^{\prime}=\tilde{p}+\frac{\gamma-\tilde{p}(\tilde{s}+1)}{2t}<\tilde{p}+\frac{1}{2}<\frac{\gamma+1}{2}\leq\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad\tilde{p}\leq u^{\prime}<\tilde{p}+1.

(2.2) Suppose
\gamma\geq 1
and
t\leq\gamma-\tilde{p}(\tilde{s}+1)<\tilde{s}+\tilde{s}/t-1
. We have

u^{\prime}=\tilde{p}+\frac{\gamma-\tilde{p}(\tilde{s}+1)+1}{2(t+1)}\leq\left\{\begin{matrix}\tilde{p}+\frac{\tilde{s}}{2t}<t+\tilde{p}+\tilde{p}\tilde{s}\leq\gamma,&\tilde{p}\geq 1\\
\frac{\gamma}{t+1}<\gamma,&\tilde{p}=0,\end{matrix}\right.

and
\tilde{p}\leq u^{\prime}<\tilde{p}+1
.

(2.3) Suppose
\gamma\geq 1
and
\tilde{s}+\tilde{s}/t-1\leq\gamma-\tilde{p}(\tilde{s}+1)<\tilde{s}+1
. We have

u^{\prime}=\tilde{p}+\frac{\gamma-\tilde{p}(\tilde{s}+1)+1-\tilde{s}}{2}<\tilde{p}+1<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad\tilde{p}\leq u^{\prime}<\tilde{p}+1.

(2.4) Suppose
\gamma<1
. We have

u^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma}{2}<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad 0\leq u^{\prime}<1.

Case III:
t<\infty
and
s<1

Denote
p:=\lfloor\gamma/(s+1)\rfloor
.

(3.1) Suppose
p(s+1)\leq\gamma<ps+p+s
. We have

u^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma+2tp-sp-p}{2t}\leq\left\{\begin{matrix}p+\frac{s}{2t}<p(s+1)\leq\gamma,&p\geq 1\\
\frac{\gamma}{2t}<\gamma,&p=0,\end{matrix}\right.

and
p\leq u^{\prime}<p+1
.

(3.2) Suppose
ps+p+s\leq\gamma<ps+p+s+1
. We have

u^{\prime}=p+\frac{s}{2t}<ps+p+s\leq\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad p\leq u^{\prime}<p+1.

Case IV:
t=\infty
and
s<1

Denote
p:=\lfloor\gamma/(s+1)\rfloor
.

(4.1) Suppose
p\geq 1
and
p(s+1)\leq\gamma<ps+p+s
. We have

u^{\prime}=p+\frac{s}{2}<p(s+1)\leq\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad p\leq u^{\prime}<p+1.

(4.2) Suppose
p\geq 1
and
ps+p+s\leq\gamma<ps+p+s+1
. We have

u^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma+p(1-s)}{2}<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad p\leq u^{\prime}<p+1.

(4.3) Suppose
\gamma<s
. We have

u^{\prime}=\gamma\cdot\min\left\{s,\frac{1}{2}\right\}<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad 0\leq u^{\prime}<1.

(4.4) Suppose
s\leq\gamma<s+1
. We have

u^{\prime}=\min\{\gamma(1+s)-s,\gamma/2\}<\gamma\quad\text{ and }\quad 0\leq u^{\prime}<1.

\blacksquare

Proposition F.5
.

For any
u\in(0,\infty]
, we have
b_{3}(u)\leq v_{2}(u)
and
b_{4}(u)<v_{2}(u)
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle b_{3}\leq

\displaystyle~2(\tilde{\ell}-\gamma)\overset{\tilde{\ell}\leq\ell_{\gamma}\leq\gamma}{\leq}\tilde{\ell}-\gamma=v_{2},

\displaystyle b_{4}\leq

\displaystyle~\tilde{\ell}-\gamma-1<v_{2}.

\blacksquare

Proposition F.6
.

Let
u^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(t)
be the optimal rate of the regularization parameter
\lambda
with qualification
t<\infty
and coefficient of source condition
s>0
defined in
49
(ignoring the logarithm term). Then, for any
u\geq u^{\prime}
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq b_{2}(u,t).

Proof.

For notational simplicity, we denote
\tilde{s}:=\min\{s,2t\}
and
b_{2}(u)=b_{2}(u,t)
for any
u\in[0,\infty]
.
We divide the proof into three steps:

(i)

We first show that for any
u^{\prime}\leq u<\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq b_{2}(u);

(ii)

We then show that we have

\max\{v_{1}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil),v_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil),b_{1}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)\}\geq b_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil);

(iii)

Finally, we show that, for any integer
q\geq 0
and any
u\geq q
, if
\max\{v_{1}(q),v_{2}(q),b_{1}(q)\}\geq b_{2}(q)
, then we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq b_{2}(u);

Combining (i), (ii), and (iii) with
q=\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil
, we obtain the desired result.

Proof of (i).

For any integer
q\geq 0
and any
q\leq q_{1}\leq q_{2}<q+1
, we have

\displaystyle v_{1}(q_{2})

\displaystyle\geq v_{1}(q_{1})

(73)

\displaystyle v_{1}(q+1)

\displaystyle=v_{1}(q)+\mathbf{1}\{q<\ell_{\gamma}\}+2(\gamma-\ell_{\gamma})\mathbf{1}\{q=\ell_{\gamma}\}

\displaystyle v_{2}(q_{2})

\displaystyle=v_{2}(q_{1})

\displaystyle v_{2}(q+1)

\displaystyle=v_{2}(q)+\mathbf{1}\{q<\ell_{\gamma}\}

\displaystyle b_{1}(q_{2})

\displaystyle=b_{1}(q_{1})

\displaystyle b_{1}(q+1)

\displaystyle=b_{1}(q)-s\mathbf{1}\{q<\ell_{\gamma}\}

\displaystyle b_{2}(q_{2})

\displaystyle\leq b_{2}(q_{1})

\displaystyle b_{2}(q+1)

\displaystyle=b_{2}(q)-\tilde{s}\mathbf{1}\{q<\ell_{\gamma}\}-2t\mathbf{1}\{q\geq\ell_{\gamma}\}.

Therefore, for any
u^{\prime}\leq u<\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil
, we have

\displaystyle\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq

\displaystyle~\max\{v_{1}(u^{\prime}),v_{2}(u^{\prime}),b_{1}(u^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Corollary D.15-17 in \cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{lu2024saturation}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}}{\geq}

\displaystyle~b_{2}(u^{\prime})\geq b_{2}(u).

Proof of (ii).

If
u^{\prime}
is an integer, then from (i) we have
\max\{v_{1}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil),v_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil),b_{1}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)\}\geq b_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)
.

Suppose
u^{\prime}
is not an integer.
From Proposition
F.4
, we have
\tilde{p}\leq u^{\prime}<\tilde{p}+1
, where
\tilde{p}:=\lfloor\gamma/(\tilde{s}+1)\rfloor
.
Denote
\tilde{\ell}_{u^{\prime}}:=\min\{\ell_{\gamma},\tilde{p}+1\}
, then we have
\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil=\tilde{p}+1
and

\displaystyle v_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)=

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}_{u^{\prime}}-\gamma

\displaystyle b_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)=

\displaystyle-2t(\tilde{p}+1)+(2t-\tilde{s})\tilde{\ell}_{u^{\prime}}.

•

If
\gamma<\tilde{p}+1
, then by the definition of
\tilde{p}\leq\ell_{\gamma}=\lfloor\gamma\rfloor
, we have
\tilde{p}=\ell_{\gamma}
and
\tilde{\ell}_{u^{\prime}}=\ell_{\gamma}
. Hence

b_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)=-2t-\tilde{s}\ell_{\gamma}\overset{t\geq 1}{<}-1<\ell_{\gamma}-\gamma=v_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil);

•

If
\gamma\geq\tilde{p}+1
, then
\tilde{\ell}_{u^{\prime}}=\tilde{p}+1
.
Since
\gamma<(\tilde{p}+1)(\tilde{s}+1)
, we have

b_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)=-\tilde{s}\tilde{p}-\tilde{s}<\tilde{p}+1-\gamma=v_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil).

Combining the above two cases, we have

\max\{v_{1}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil),v_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil),b_{1}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil)\}\geq b_{2}(\lceil u^{\prime}\rceil).

Proof of (iii).

Suppose that for some integer
q\geq 0
, we have
\max\{v_{1}(q),v_{2}(q),b_{1}(q)\}\geq b_{2}(q)
.
By (i), for any
q^{\prime\prime}\in[q,q+1)
we have

\displaystyle\max\{v_{1}(q^{\prime\prime}),v_{2}(q^{\prime\prime}),b_{1}(q^{\prime\prime})\}

\displaystyle\geq b_{2}(q^{\prime\prime}).

(74)

Thus, the inequality holds throughout the interval
[q,q+1)
.

Next, consider the integer point
q+1
. We analyze three possible cases:

•

If
s\leq 2t
, then
\tilde{s}=s
and (
73
) implies

\displaystyle~\max\{v_{1}(q+1),v_{2}(q+1),b_{1}(q+1)\}\geq\max\{v_{1}(q),v_{2}(q),b_{1}(q)\}-s\mathbf{1}\{q<\ell_{\gamma}\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~b_{2}(q)-s\mathbf{1}\{q<\ell_{\gamma}\}\geq b_{2}(q+1).

•

If
s>2t
and
q<\ell_{\gamma}
, then from (
67
) we have
b_{1}(q)=-s(q+1)<-2tq=b_{2}(q)
, so the inequality
\max\{v_{1}(q),v_{2}(q),b_{1}(q)\}\geq b_{2}(q)
must come from the first two components:

\max\{v_{1}(q),v_{2}(q)\}\geq b_{2}(q).

Therefore, (
73
) implies

\displaystyle~\max\{v_{1}(q+1),v_{2}(q+1),b_{1}(q+1)\}\geq\max\{v_{1}(q+1),v_{2}(q+1)\}\geq\max\{v_{1}(q),v_{2}(q)\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~b_{2}(q)\geq b_{2}(q+1).

•

If
s>2t
and
q\geq\ell_{\gamma}
, then (
73
) implies

\displaystyle~\max\{v_{1}(q+1),v_{2}(q+1),b_{1}(q+1)\}\geq\max\{v_{1}(q),v_{2}(q),b_{1}(q)\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~b_{2}(q)\geq b_{2}(q+1).

Combining (
74
) and all three cases above, we conclude that if

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq b_{2}(u),

holds at integer
u=q
, it also holds for all
u\in[q,q+1]
.

Finally, by induction over integers
q+1,q+2,\cdots
, we have

\max\{v_{1}(u),v_{2}(u),b_{1}(u)\}\geq b_{2}(u),\quad u\geq q,

which completes the proof of (iii).

\blacksquare

Appendix G
Kernel approximation results for kernels on sphere

In this section, we will restate and develop several approximation results for kernels defined on the sphere (see, e.g., Section 2.1).

Throughout this section, we assume that all conditions and assumptions in Theorem 3.1 hold.

The following lemma is borrowed from Proposition 3 in
31
as well as Lemma S5 in
77
.

Lemma G.1
.

We have

\displaystyle\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}+\Delta_{1}\right),\quad\|\Delta_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)

(75)

\displaystyle\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}\Sigma_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{2}\Psi_{>\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{2}\left(\mathrm{I}_{n}+\Delta_{2}\right),\quad\|\Delta_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1);

with

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}=\Theta_{d}(1),\quad\kappa_{2}=\Theta_{d}\left(d^{-(\ell_{\gamma}+1)}\right).

The following two lemmas are borrowed from
31
and
69
. Readers can also find the first lemma in Lemma S6 of
77
.

Lemma G.2

(Lemma 11 in
31
; Lemma S6 of
77
)
.

When
\gamma>\ell_{\gamma}
, we have

\left\|\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}-\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\gamma}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1).

Lemma G.3

(Theorem 1 in
69
)
.

Suppose
\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
. Denote
N_{\ell_{\gamma}-1}=\sum_{k^{\prime}=0}^{\ell_{\gamma}-1}N(d,k^{\prime})
, then

\left\|\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}-1}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}-1}}{n}-\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\gamma}-1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1).

(76)

And we have

\lambda_{\text{max}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\ell_{\gamma}}\right)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda_{\text{min}\{n,N(d,\ell_{\gamma})\}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\ell_{\gamma}}\right)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1),

hence

\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Psi_{\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\ell_{\gamma}}\right)=\Theta_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1).

Remark G.4
.

For completeness, we briefly explain how to obtain Lemma
G.2
and Lemma
G.3
from Lemma 11 in
31
and Theorem 1 in
69
.
A comparison of notations is given in Table
1
.
Recall

•

When
\gamma>\ell_{\gamma}
, Proposition 2.1 implies that
n/(N_{\ell_{\gamma}}\log(N_{\ell_{\gamma}}))=\Theta_{d}(d^{\gamma-\ell_{\gamma}}\log^{-1}(d))\to\infty
as
d\to\infty
.
Denote
\Delta=\frac{\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}^{\top}\Psi_{\leq\ell_{\gamma}}}{n}-\mathrm{I}_{N_{\ell_{\gamma}}}
, then Lemma 11 in
31
implies
\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}]=o_{d}(1)
, and Markov’s inequality implies
\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

•

When
\gamma=\ell_{\gamma}
, following the proof of Lemma 11 in
31
with
N_{\ell_{\gamma}}
being replaced by
N_{\ell_{\gamma}-1}
, we can similarly show (
76
). The remaining part of Lemma
G.3
is a direct result of Theorem 1 in
69
.
